In its center, again, imagine the body, speech, and mind of the Tathāgata, transcending all worlds, having perfectly gone beyond the ground of mind and attained the ground of mind, bestowing the special fruits that are desired.

"To its east is the tathāgata Ratnaketu. To the south is the tathāgata Saṃkusumitarājendra. To the north is the tathāgata Dundubhisvara. To the west is the tathāgata Amitābha. In the southeastern quarter is the bodhisattva Samantabhadra. In the northeastern quarter is Avalokiteśvara. In the southwestern quarter is Mañjuśrī Kumārabhūta. In the northwestern quarter is Maitreya. On all the anthers draw the mothers of the bodhisattvas, the six perfections, and the samādhis. Below, draw the vidyā-mantras and the hosts of wrathful ones. On the stalk is Vajrapāṇi. All the rest is the great ocean.

Lamps, flowers, and incense,
Food offerings and fragrant smoke, arisen from the mind:
For the sake of the pledge of embodied beings,
The mantrin should imagine them all.
And on all the ground below,
The countless gods and the rest
In this maṇḍala of the great being
The mantrin should carefully generate and draw.
Having thoroughly purified his own body
By himself with the syllable called ra,
The mantrin sits upon the seat of yoga,
Recollecting the tathāgatas.
On the crowns of the disciples' heads
He places the syllable a together with the anusvāra;
Then, fearless in his own person,
He has them cast a flower.
Having examined them, he should teach them,
Or else he may give them a vidyā-mantra.
For the sake of the pledge of embodied beings,
This is held to be the chief maṇḍala.

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, this is the extensive chapter on the procedure for leading disciples into the secret maṇḍala.

Then Vajrapāṇi, for the sake of those who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra, asked the Blessed One Vairocana in melodious verse about the observance of the vidyā-mantra:

"How is the observance to be practiced?
How does one abide in moral discipline?
Wherever and however one may abide,
How does one act without attachment?
When how much time has passed
Does the observance become complete?
Please tell me what its power is,
And where it is to be carried out.
Free of time, place, and activity,
Rid of Dharma and what is not Dharma,
How does it come about swiftly?
Guide, please declare its measure too,
That by which siddhi is attained,
As the buddhas taught it in the past,
For the sake of the beings of the future.
Perfect Buddha, chief of those who walk on two feet,
I ask you, the Omniscient One;
Chief of the victors, be my witness."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One,
The Great Vairocana, at that time,
Out of love for sentient beings,
Spoke these words:
"Vajrapāṇi of great might,
Great being, excellent hero,
Keep firmly the sublime observance
Taught by the buddhas of the past.
That by which siddhi is attained
Which brings benefit to the world:
The observance arisen from the vidyā observance;
Abide in it as the buddhas do.
Having generated one's own reality,
The mind ever free of doubt,
When one is constantly in meditative equipoise,
Then the observance is practiced.
When the awakening mind, the dharmas,
The training, action, and whatever result there is
Come to possess a single characteristic,
The moral discipline that has abandoned deeds and activity
Is called discipline like that of the buddhas;
Otherwise one is not called endowed with discipline.
When one has gained mastery over all dharmas,
Has grasped the realization of the aims of beings,
And gold and stone have become the same,
One swiftly acts without attachment.
From among the counts of secret mantra,
However many hundred thousands are taught,
When that much time has passed,
The observance becomes complete.
In the manner of Mahendra,
Abiding in a Mahendra maṇḍala,
Binding the vajra-holder's seal,
One should always drink milk as one's food;
Fully restraining breath and exertion,
The mantrin should recite for one month.
Then, in the second month,
Endowed with the water maṇḍala,
Having bound the lotus mudrā,
One drinks water as one's food at that time.
Then, in the third month,
Intent upon the aspect of fire,
Eating food one has not taken up,
And endowed with the sword mudrā,
One should burn away all the wrongdoing
Arisen from body, speech, and mind.
In the fourth, being joined with wind,
One should always take wind as one's food.
Then one binds the wheel mudrā,
And in equipoise recites the secret mantra.
Having remained intent in yoga
Upon the aspects of Mahendra and of water,
In the waxing fortnight of the moon,
Then, in the fifth month,
Having abandoned gain and lack of gain,
When the mantrin is wholly without attachment,
He becomes like a perfect buddha.
Having joined himself with wind and fire,
Free of every fault,
Having abandoned gain and lack of gain,
He should recite in a further month.
Śakra, Brahmā, and the other gods,
Piśācas, mahoragas, and rākṣasas
Will bow to him from afar,
And all of them will protect him as well.
All of them will heed his word
And will act according to his command.
The medicinal herbs, humans, and gods,
The vidyā-mantras and all the secret mantras
Will say, 'What shall we do?'
And will remain in his presence.
Obstructors, malicious vināyakas,
Rākṣasas, and the mātṛkās,
When they see the holder of secret mantra,
Will bow to him from afar.
Wherever the knower of mantra dwells,
Abiding in the vidyā observance,
That place blazes like a fire
Of utter destruction and becomes fearsome to them.
He attains every rank of lordship;
Gentle one, like a perfect buddha,
He is the glorious tamer of those hard to tame,
A great vajra holder, an ascetic,
Bringing benefit to all beings,
Compassionate like Lokeśvara.
When six months have passed,
He attains the desired fruit of siddhi,
And he always brings benefit
To himself and to others as well."

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, this is the extensive chapter on the observance of the vidyā-mantra.

Then again Vajrapāṇi asked the Blessed One about the essence of the secret mantras and of the maṇḍalas:

"What is the essence of all the secret mantras?
Knowing what does one become a master?"
When he had made this request, the Blessed One,
The Great Vairocana, at that time
Said to Vajradhara:
"Excellent, excellent, great being!
More secret than the secret of wisdom,
That from which the wisdom of secret mantra greatly arises
I shall explain to you, my son;
Listen with an undistracted mind.
The syllable a is rightly taught
To be the essence of all the secret mantras.
The secret mantras that arise from it,
Countless as they are, come forth;
And all the wisdom that pacifies
Every conceptual elaboration is born."

"Lord of Secrets, how is the syllable a the essence of all the secret mantras?

"The perfect Buddha, chief of those who walk on two feet,
Has said that a is the chief.
Therefore all of this, everything,
Abides throughout all the limbs.
The limbs too, as is fitting,
Always come to know in every way.
The letters pervade everything,
And the letters too are pervaded by it.
Within the letters sound arises,
Arising from part upon part.
Therefore all of this, everything,
The body, arises from variety.
Its arrangement has been rightly taught;
Gentle one, listen to the essences.
Having placed the essence at the heart,
Set the limbs upon the limbs.
The wise one, seated upon the seat of yoga,
Makes his own body into the body of a perfect buddha,
And recollecting the tathāgatas,
Should do all of this.
When one comes to know the vast wisdom
In accordance with the rite,
Then the buddhas of great power
Declare that one to be a master.
Therefore that one is the Tathāgata;
That one is called the perfect Buddha.
The buddhas and the bodhisattvas,
Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Maheśvara,
The Sun, the Moon, Varuṇa,
Śakra, and the Lord of Creatures,
Time and Yama too are that one;
He is Pṛthivī, the earth goddess, and Sarasvatī;
He is a brahmin and one who has performed the ritual bath,
And he is also called a celibate practitioner;
A glorious monk whose defilements are exhausted,
The holder of all secrets,
The Omniscient One and the All-Seeing One,
The chief lord of all dharmas;
One who is steadfast in the awakening mind,
Who is devoted to knowing sound,
Unattached to all dharmas,
Should be known as knowing all in every aspect.
The one on whose body
All the wheels of letters abide
Is the mantrin, the holder of secret mantra,
The glorious king of mantra, Vajradhara.
In the middle between the brows, the syllable hūṃ
Is the ground of the Vajra Holder.
At the chest is the syllable called sa,
Which is called the ground of Padmapāṇi.
I am placed at the place of the heart,
The lord of all, the Sovereign.
With all the variety of moving and unmoving things
Everything without exception is pervaded by me.
The one called a is the supreme life;
The one called va is taught to be speech;
The one called ra is said to be fire;
The one called hūṃ is taught to be the wrathful one;
kha should be known as space;
The anusvāra is called the supreme of emptiness.
One who knows this is called a master;
This is the truly sublime.
Therefore, in every way,
If one always strives at this
Which is called the mantra of the perfect Buddha,
One attains the state free of death and transmigration."

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, this is the chapter on knowing the reality of the master.

How the letters are to be set out
Has been well taught by the perfect Buddha;
Therefore listen to it in full.
ka should be known to be at the gullet;
kha should be placed at the palate;
ga should be known to be at the throat;
gha is placed at the place of the neck.
ca should be placed on the tongue;
cha is placed at the middle of the tongue;
ja at the tip of the tongue;
jha at the root of the tongue.
ṭa should be known to be at the calves;
ṭha should be placed at the thighs;
ḍa is taught to be placed at the waist;
ḍha is placed at the two feet.
ta is taught to be placed at the bladder;
tha should be known to be at the belly;
da is placed at the two armpits;
dha is placed at the two sides of the ribs.
pa should be known to be at the back;
pha should be known to be at the chest;
ba is placed at the two shoulders;
bha is placed at the two hands;
ma is placed at the seat of the mind;
Then it should be known properly.
ya is said to be the male organ;
ra is taught to be the eyes;
la, which should be known as vast,
Is the forehead of that protector.
i ī are said to be the two eyes;
u ū are taught to be the armpits;
e ai should be known as the two ears;
o au are held to be the two cheeks.
aṃ is the state of the perfect Buddha;
aḥ is nirvāṇa.
Having come to know all of this according to the rite,
Through the supreme observance become a buddha!
The one in whose heart the treasure
Of the wealth of omniscience always abides
Knows all things in the world;
Gentle one, he is like the Omniscient One.

From The Manifest and Complete Awakening of the Great Vairocana, His Miraculous Manifestation and Empowerment, this is the chapter on the arrangement of the letters.

Then again Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, please explain the bases of training of the bodhisattva great beings, embraced by skillful means and wisdom, endowed with which the bodhisattva great beings will be free of doubt and hesitation and will not decline while they circle in saṃsāra."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One Vairocana looked upon the entire dharmadhātu with the eye of a tathāgata and said this to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Lord of Secrets, listen to the teaching on skill in the way of the bodhisattva's conduct, abiding in which the bodhisattva great beings will come to comprehend the realization of the Great Vehicle.

"Lord of Secrets, in this regard the bodhisattva should turn away from killing, from taking what is not given, and from sexual misconduct. He should turn away from lying, harsh speech, slander, and idle chatter. He should turn away from covetousness, malice, and wrong view. Lord of Secrets, these are the trainings of the bodhisattva, training in which one trains in conduct equal to that of the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattvas."

Then again Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, said this to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, the Blessed One has also taught these ten paths of virtuous action in the Śrāvaka Vehicle. Blessed One, since worldly non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) too take up these ten paths of virtuous action and abide in them, Blessed One, what distinction is there in this? What difference is there?"

When he had asked this, the Blessed One said to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Excellent, excellent, Lord of Secrets! Lord of Secrets, that you have asked the Tathāgata about this matter, Lord of Secrets, you have done well. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, listen to the Dharma discourse that teaches the mode of thorough differentiation and the single mode.

"Lord of Secrets, the training that I have taught to the śrāvakas is not endowed with skillful means and wisdom; it carries out what has been commanded, and it is the realization of a partial knowledge. Therefore it and these ten paths of virtuous action are not the same. Worldly people engage in them out of attachment to things as their own, and on account of conditions supplied by others. Bodhisattvas who go by the Great Vehicle, because they enter into the sameness of all phenomena, do not depend on others; embraced by skillful means and wisdom, they engage in accomplishing the benefit of themselves, of others, and of both. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, bodhisattva great beings should strive to engage in all phenomena embraced by wisdom and skillful means."

Then the Blessed One, having looked upon the realms of sentient beings with the eyes of great loving-kindness and great compassion, said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "In this regard the bodhisattva should turn away from deliberately taking life. He should lay down the rod and give up enmity. He should protect the lives of others just as he does his own. The exception is when, toward such a person and in such a circumstance, he takes the evil upon himself and performs that act; and even that he should do without enmity, with a mind that has become compassion.

"He should turn away from taking what is not given. He should not even give rise to attachment toward the wealth that others possess, so what need is there to speak of taking others' wealth without its being given? The exception is when he sees sentient beings who are miserly and stingy, who do not give and do not make merit: what he does toward such persons in order to rid them of miserliness. Then he himself, without any attachment to it, should give gifts on their behalf. From time to time he should speak in praise of giving. If the bodhisattva were to give rise to attachment or desire for that wealth, Lord of Secrets, know that the bodhisattva has fallen away from the limbs of awakening and has incurred a transgression that cannot be remedied in the Dharma and Vinaya.

"He should turn away from sexual misconduct. He should not even give rise to a mind of desire toward women who are the wives of others, or who are protected by their family, by a sign, or by the Dharma; so what need is there to speak of engaging with what is not the proper organ, or of joining the two organs? The exception is acting in such circumstances.

"He should turn away from lying. Deliberately, even for the sake of his life, he should not speak a false word by which the awakening of the buddhas would be betrayed. For such a transgression against the awakening of the buddhas, Lord of Secrets, is the great lie of a bodhisattva who abides in the Great Vehicle. Lord of Secrets, by this explanation too you should understand that one should never abandon truthful speech in this way.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, he should turn away from harsh speech. He should speak to sentient beings words that are gentle, soft, and pleasing to the mind. Why is that? Because, Lord of Secrets, soft speech is what comes first when the bodhisattva acts for the benefit of all sentient beings. The exception is the bodhisattva's speaking harshly, in those various situations, to sentient beings who bear the causes of terrible destinies.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, he should turn away from slander. He should act in such a way that there is no division and no harm. Bodhisattvas are not engaged in dividing sentient beings. The exception is when he sees sentient beings sunk in such standpoints of view: dividing them in such a manner that those beings come to be firmly established in this single mode of omniscience.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, the bodhisattva should turn away from idle chatter. He should speak words that suit the place, the purpose, and the time, that follow the meaning, that are pleasing to all sentient beings, and that move along the pathway of the ear. Why is that? Because the bodhisattva is endowed with many kinds of speech. The exception is when the bodhisattva, beginning with telling stories, delights sentient beings and, wishing to establish them in the Dharma of the Buddha, speaks even words that have no purpose. In that way, even in saṃsāra, the bodhisattva's cause will continue without interruption.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, the bodhisattva should turn away from covetousness. He should not even give rise to a mind attached to the enjoyments of others or the wealth of others. Why is that? Because the bodhisattva is not faint-hearted. If a bodhisattva's mind becomes attached on his own account, he falls away from one part of the gateway of omniscience. Lord of Secrets, the bodhisattva should give rise to delight and think in this way: 'That they have themselves become prosperous in what I would have had to do for them is excellent!' Thinking this, he should give rise again and again to joy that the enjoyments of those beings are not wasted."

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, he should turn away from malice. In every situation he should be abundant in patience and loving-kindness, free of anger, and even-minded. Just as he is toward a friend, so too he should be toward an enemy. Why is that? Because the bodhisattva's mind is not possessed of dysfunction; the bodhisattva is by nature of completely pure intention. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, the bodhisattva should be free of malice.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, the bodhisattva should be without wrong view. With right view, with a view that sees the dangers of the world beyond, without deceit, without crookedness, without guile, and upright, he should be of settled conviction toward the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha. Why is that? Lord of Secrets, wrong view is the great fault of a bodhisattva; it cuts off all the roots of virtue. It is the mother of all the roots of non-virtue. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, one should not give rise to wrong view even in jest."

Then again Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, please explain how bodhisattvas do not let their vow of these ten paths of virtuous action decline or be cut off at the very root; and, Blessed One, how a bodhisattva, even while surrounded by royal sovereignty, household, wife, sons, daughters, relatives, and blood kin, and while experiencing the happiness of gods and humans, does not incur a transgression."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Excellent, excellent, Lord of Secrets! Therefore, Lord of Secrets, listen and keep it well in mind, and I shall explain skill in the precise determination of transgressions in the discipline of bodhisattvas.

"Lord of Secrets, bodhisattvas are of two kinds. What are the two? Householders and those who have gone forth. Of these, the householder bodhisattva dwells in the household, takes up the five bases of training, and exercises sovereignty over a kingdom by various means and methods.

"Lord of Secrets, the bodhisattva who wishes to attain omniscience in accordance with place and time, embraced by skillful means, displays the various fields of craft, such as song, music, and conjuring, and through those various means and methods gathers sentient beings by the four means of attraction; and that, too, is for the sake of seeking unsurpassed, perfect awakening. In this he has entirely renounced taking life, taking what is not given, sexual misconduct, lying, and wrong view. The householder bodhisattva, having taken up those five bases of training and training in the trainings just as they have been taught, should with faith train in the footsteps of the tathāgatas of the past.

"Abiding in the unsurpassed, unconditioned aggregate of moral discipline praised by the Tathāgata, he practices conditioned moral discipline embraced by skillful means and wisdom; and the four root transgressions he should never violate, even for the sake of his life. What are the four? They are abandoning the true Dharma, giving up the awakening mind, being miserly, and harming sentient beings. Why is that? Because these are not endowed with skillful means and wisdom, are by nature afflicted, and cannot be restored. Why is that?

"The buddhas who have passed beyond,
Likewise those who have not yet come,
And the protectors of the present,
Having trained in what is endowed with means and wisdom,
Attain that unsurpassed awakening,
Which is unconditioned.
The teaching of wisdom and of trainings
Not endowed with skillful means,
Great hero, was given to the śrāvakas
In order to draw them into it."

This concludes the chapter on the bodhisattva's training endowed with skillful means, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

The seventh section.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana looked upon the assembled retinue, complete in every respect, and, wishing the teaching to be fruitfully accomplished, in order to explain the lord of all secret mantras, the king of secret mantras, the protector of all secret mantras, of great splendor, abiding in the three pledges, who brings the three paths to perfection,

He spoke with gentle voice
To Vajrapāṇi of great strength:
"Protector of secret mantras among secret mantras,
Hero, listen with undistracted mind."

At that time, having entered into the absorption called Sublime Wisdom, he uttered this secret mantra, the source of the various wisdoms, radiating a hundred rays of light:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ aṃ

"Vajrapāṇi,
This is the protector of all secret mantras,
Of great splendor;
This is the very self of the perfect Buddha,
The Sage, lord of all phenomena.
It destroys all the black darkness of ignorance,
And shines like the risen sun.
The great Sage in his very self
Has empowered it in just this way,
And, wishing to benefit sentient beings,
He emanates emanations.
However many heroic beings arise
Among purified sentient beings,
For that many will it accomplish
The sublime state of emanation.
Therefore, in every respect,
Those who seek the means of perfect buddhahood,
With their inner self purified,
Should always strive with diligence."

This concludes the chapter teaching the arising of the hundred syllables, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana said to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Lord of Secrets, when the buddhas, the blessed ones, attain the ground endowed with the empowerment of the superknowledge of great wisdom, at that time those buddhas, the blessed ones, see themselves abiding on the path of the three pledges. Lord of Secrets, at that time too, that Blessed One, endowed with the empowerment of the superknowledge of great wisdom, displays the activity of a buddha in the manner of a dhāraṇī."

"At that very time, through the activity of a buddha, those buddhas, the blessed ones, become present to all sentient beings as teachers of the path of the three pledges.

"Lord of Secrets, consider the gateway of the maṇḍala of my wheel of speech: a vast emanation pervading endless world systems, a gateway of purity, which satisfies the hopes of all sentient beings just as they are, a gateway by which the realm of phenomena is individually discerned. Even now, although the Blessed One Śākyamuni is engaged in performing the deeds of a buddha in all the world systems, without exception, as boundless as the realm of space, Lord of Secrets, look how sentient beings do not recognize that 'this is the arising of the maṇḍala of the Blessed One's wheel of speech; this is the arising of the body of a buddha, from which springs the essence of the ornament of the array of the Buddha's speech, which satisfies sentient beings just as they hope.'

"Nevertheless, the bodhisattvas who strive to awaken fully to the realm of phenomena through gateways as many as the oceans of endless world systems, those who have arisen from the conduct of the bodhisattva Samantabhadra, here in this ocean of world systems adorned with the essence of the flower base, held in my embrace, purify buddhafields through gateways purified of various intentions, accomplish the seat of awakening, fully awaken into awakening, turn the wheel of Dharma, and empower the deeds of a buddha. Beyond that, seeking the state of a perfectly complete buddha, because the mind is immeasurable, the body is immeasurable; because the body is immeasurable, wisdom is immeasurable; because wisdom is immeasurable, sentient beings are immeasurable; and because sentient beings are immeasurable, they attain the immeasurable realm of space.

"Lord of Secrets, in this way, through immeasurable sentient beings, they attain the four immeasurables. Having attained them, endowed with the strength of the ten powers, victorious over the four māras, sounding the roar of the bull and the lion, they become perfectly complete buddhas.

"Having recited this essence of a hundred gateways
Spoken by the perfect Buddha,
Those heroes come to realize
All these sublime states."

This concludes the chapter teaching the application of the result, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then again Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, said this to the Blessed One:

"This explanation of the protector of mantras,
The source of mantras among mantras,
Is a great wonder, Blessed One.
Great Sage, in what place,
By what means, and how should it be known?
How the mantras arise from it,
By what and in what way, tell me.
Protector, supreme among speakers,
Please explain all of this to me."

When he had requested this, the Blessed One,
Lord of Dharma, the great Sage,
The omniscient Blessed One, Vairocana,
At that time, with words
That pervaded all the world systems,
Spoke with gentle voice:
"Vajrapāṇi of great might,
Great being, excellent, excellent!
The supreme secret of the buddhas,
Greatly wondrous, deeply hidden,
Unknown to all logicians,
All this I shall explain to you.

"Those empowered in the maṇḍala of the Great Vehicle,
The source of compassion,
Those who are upright and gentle,
And always of great compassion,
Those who never look upon awakening
As having an object:
Their essence
Will be known as the great self.

"The place where the Guide resides
Is the heart:
A lotus arisen from the mind,
Eight-petaled and most beautiful,
And on it a disk like the moon,
Like a stainless mirror.
There the protector of secret mantras,
Of great might, abides forever,
Golden in color and ablaze,
Abiding in a samādhi free from evil,
Hard to gaze upon, like the sun.
By it all these beings,
Both outside and within,
Are empowered so that it pervades them all.

"Know the mirror to be the mind;
The eye of wisdom is likewise.
When the mantra practitioner gazes
Into the mirror with the eye of wisdom,
He sees his own form as the perfect Buddha,
With characteristics utterly at peace.
Then on the body, the form of a body,
And in the mind, a form arisen from mind,
Pure and wholly beautiful,
Come forth through one's own action.
Then its rays of light,
By which the mantra practitioner
Accomplishes all the conduct of a buddha,
Shine out everywhere.
Likewise, by a purified self,
Through gazing or through speaking,
Every aim held in mind
Will indeed be accomplished."

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva practicing the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra should generate on the body the form of a body in this way. There is nothing superior to the perfectly complete Buddha. Just as one's own eyes, ears, nose, tongue, body, mind, and the rest are included within the four great elements, and these in turn are empty of essential nature, comprised by mere names, equal to and like space, ungraspable, arisen from causes and karma, like reflections, in just the same way the tathāgatas fully awakened. These too are linked in an unbroken stream of mutual dependent origination; whatever is born in dependence arises like a reflection. Therefore, because of mutual dependent origination, one should think, 'Whatever is the deity, that is myself; whatever I am, that is the deity,' and in this way generate the form of one's body as the body of the deity.

"Lord of Secrets, see how realization arises in dependence on the Dharma, and the Dharma arises in dependence on realization. Because both are empty of intrinsic nature, they are not even posited as agent and action with respect to each other.

"Lord of Secrets, if one asks how one should generate in the mind a form arisen from mind: Lord of Secrets, it is like this. If one brings to mind white, yellow, or red, the mind becomes attached to that consciousness. Therefore, by such a color one accomplishes only purposes of that same kind."

"Lord of Secrets, it is like this:

When one has visualized the maṇḍala in the mind
And places it upon someone with a fever,
In that very moment the person
Will be freed from the fever, without a doubt.

"Without the mind there is no maṇḍala, and without the maṇḍala there is no mind. Why is that? Because the maṇḍala and the mind are of one characteristic.

"Lord of Secrets, it is like this: if a magician were to have one illusory man conjure up another man, Lord of Secrets, what do you think? What difference would there be between them?"

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, said this to the Blessed One: "There is no difference at all between those two men. Blessed One, why is that? Because both men have arisen from what is not real, and because illusion is empty of intrinsic nature."

"Lord of Secrets, in the same way, both the thing arisen from mind and the mind itself are imagined, and therefore they are empty of essential nature."

This concludes the chapter on the practice of arranging the hundred syllables, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then again the Blessed One said to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Lord of Secrets, listen to how the body of the protector of secret mantras and one's own body are inseparable.

"Arisen from what the mind has become,
And with the mind wholly purified,
When the protector dwells there,
Rays of light springing from his body
Radiate constantly in every direction
From each of his limbs.
Not knowing these modes,
The deluded do not understand them.
However many kinds of body there are,
They are more than can be told;
Likewise the kinds of the protector of mantras
Are said to be countless.
Just as the wish-fulfilling jewel,
Glorious, accomplishes the aims one desires,
So too the body of the lamp of the world
Accomplishes every aim."

"Lord of Secrets, if one asks how the nonconceptual realm of phenomena accomplishes every purpose: Lord of Secrets, it is like this, for example. The realm of space is not a sentient being, not a life principle, not a human, not an agent, not an experiencer, not something grasped and not a grasper, and it is free of all thought and conceptualization; and yet within it all the realms of sentient beings without exception carry out everything, going, coming, and the rest. Of this there is no doubt.

"Likewise this omniscience,
Nonconceptual and without abode,
Accomplishes with certainty
Everything for beings, outer and inner."

Then again the Blessed One, with words that range through all the realms of sentient beings without exception, spoke words for accomplishing samādhi, words of the gateway that transforms into inconceivable words:

"When from the very beginning
Nothing at all arises or perishes,
How then does the wisdom of emptiness
Arise for yogins?
When the thing called 'phenomenon'
Is not found by its essential nature,
Then the mind like space,
The source of awakening, will arise.
Then too compassion
In harmony with all the world will arise.
Abiding in the mode of mere names,
'Buddha' is here spoken as a name.
Then 'empty' and 'not empty'
Are truly designated as names.
Then the divisions of number,
One, two, three, and so on, also arise.
Thus, hero, that emptiness,
Through the empowerment of the self-arisen ones,
Increases in order, beginning with a:

ba ka kha ga gha ca cha ja jha ṭa ṭha ḍa ḍha ta tha da dha pa pha ba bha ma ya ra la va śa ṣa sa ha ṅa ña ṇa na."

"Lord of Secrets, with these, behold the mode of accomplishing samādhi through the empowering might of emptiness, empowered by the sign called a. Lord of Secrets, that very a abides as the self arrayed in various forms. It manifestly shows its own essential nature as 'All phenomena are unborn.'

"In some cases it teaches through the form of ba, meaning the inexpressible. In some cases it teaches through the form of ka, meaning that all phenomena are free of purpose. In some cases it teaches through the form of kha, 'All phenomena are like space.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ga, 'In all phenomena there is no going.' In some cases it teaches through the form of gha, 'All phenomena are a manifold massed into one.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ca, 'All phenomena are without death and transmigration.' In some cases it teaches through the form of cha, 'All phenomena are like autumn clouds.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ja, 'All phenomena are without birth.' In some cases it teaches through the form of jha, 'All phenomena are free of turbidity.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ṭa, 'All phenomena are free of pride.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ṭha, 'All phenomena are free of production.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ḍa, 'All phenomena are free of confusion.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ḍha, 'All phenomena are free of deception.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ta, 'All phenomena are free of suchness.' In some cases it teaches through the form of tha, 'All phenomena are free of a place.' In some cases it teaches through the form of da, 'All phenomena are free of giving.' In some cases it teaches through the form of dha, 'In all phenomena an element cannot be observed.' In some cases it teaches through the form of pa, 'In all phenomena ultimate truth cannot be observed.' In some cases it teaches through the form of pha, 'All phenomena are without core, like foam.' In some cases it teaches through the form of pa, 'In all phenomena existence cannot be observed.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ma, 'In all phenomena death cannot be observed.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ya, 'In all phenomena a vehicle cannot be observed.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ra, 'All phenomena are free of dust.' In some cases it teaches through the form of la, 'In all phenomena characteristics cannot be observed.' In some cases it teaches through the form of śa, 'All phenomena are free of peace.' In some cases it teaches through the form of ṣa, 'All phenomena are by nature inert.' In some cases it teaches through the form of sa, 'In all phenomena truth cannot be observed.'

"In some cases it teaches through the form of ha, 'All phenomena are free of cause.'

"Lord of Secrets, in this way, from each of these gateways for accomplishing samādhi, one should understand thirty-two gateways for accomplishing samādhi. Lord of Secrets, see how the thirty-two marks of a great being also arise from these very letters. ṇa, ña, ṇa, and na have mastery over all samādhis; see how from them the excellent minor marks of the perfectly complete Buddha are also accomplished.

"Therefore, Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva practicing the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra should understand that in this way the culmination of the Buddha's intent, the pledge of the arising of the hundred syllables, has been taught in the measure of the ten minds multiplied tenfold.

"The perfect Buddha has encompassed
And expanded all of these;
Since all of them are present in me as well,
I am the supreme Victor, all the buddhas."

This concludes the chapter on accomplishing oneself, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

"Furthermore, Lord of Secrets, all of these are words for accomplishing the mode of samādhi. When one is empowered by this emptiness, one becomes just like the perfect Buddha, who has mastery over all phenomena.

"Therefore the letter is the deity:
The one called a is the supreme state.
Encircled by the letters,
It shines forth everywhere.
That deity is without characteristics,
Having abandoned both the basis of characterization and the characteristic.
From the deity without characteristics
These characteristics have arisen.
From sound the letters arose;
From the letters the secret mantras arose;
And that the result arises from the mantras
All the protectors have declared.
Sound should be known as empty;
Just as it is, in all kinds of beings,
Free of conceptual thought,
So all of this is empty.
Yet sound that is not empty also exists,
And its applications too are taught throughout.
When that sound has been realized,
The abode of the deity and samādhi,
The joining of the arrangement of letters,
And all things taught by means of the letters,
Become the basis for designating
The convention 'the many secret mantras.'"

This concludes the chapter on the mantra rite of the hundred syllables, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

"Just as space never abides
In any of the directions,
So the protector of secret mantras
Abides in no phenomenon at all.
Just as things abide in space
And always shine forth there,
Yet space does not become their abode,
So too is the protector of secret mantras.
Just as space is held to be
Free of the three times and the rest,
So the protector of secret mantras,
Though without the three times, shines forth.
A basis for designation as mere name,
Free of agent and the like:
So the wise designate space
By way of convention.
Apart from the name, nothing whatsoever
Exists, just as with space;
So too the lord of secret mantras
Merely appears as convention.
He is not fire, nor water, nor wind,
Nor earth, nor the sun,
Nor the moon or any of the planets,
Neither day nor night,
Neither birth nor cessation,
Neither death nor decline,
Not an instant, not a moment,
Not a year or the like,
Neither destruction nor formation;
No count of eons exists for him,
Nor is there any fruit
Arisen from virtue and non-virtue.
One who seeks the state of omniscience
Should always strive in that
Where all the various kinds
Of conceptual thought are absent."

This concludes the chapter teaching the essential nature of perfect awakening, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, of what Dharma is this expression 'the three pledges' a designation?"

When he had asked this, the Blessed One Vairocana said to Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets: "Lord of Secrets, what you have asked me is excellent, excellent! Therefore listen and keep it well in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

Saying, "Blessed One, so be it," Vajrapāṇi, the Lord of Secrets, listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One said this to him: "That special quality of the Dharma from which three kinds of special quality of the Dharma come forth in an unobstructed way is the three pledges. And what is that special quality of the Dharma? What is called Dharma is the first mind: the nature of what has been cultivated, together with the wisdom that arises subsequent to it; and, having fully understood reality just as it is by that wisdom, generating the mind free of the entire net of conceptual thought is the characteristic of awakening. The second is nonconceptuality: the state of the perfect Buddha. Lord of Secrets, having seen reality just as it is, and having looked upon all the realms of sentient beings without exception, one comes under the power of compassion, and there arises the awakening mind that thinks, 'I shall establish sentient beings in that very awakening which cannot be objectified, which is free of all conceptual elaboration and without characteristics.' That is the three pledges.

"Lord of Secrets, furthermore, what are the three pledges?

"The first mind is the Buddha;
The second is called the Dharma;
The mind that arises together with it
Is explained as the Saṅgha.
Hero, these three pledges
The guides have explained to you.
Then, through gateways of various modes,
The hero who wishes to benefit beings,
Abiding in these three pledges,
Practices the conduct of awakening.

"Lord of Secrets, when those who have mastery over the three bodies have become perfect buddhas, at that time those perfectly complete buddhas first empower a single emanation body and establish the teaching. Lord of Secrets, furthermore, that single body is set forth as three: the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha. Lord of Secrets, furthermore, it is taught by being set forth as the three vehicles.

At that time, having done all the deeds of a buddha, they display complete nirvāṇa and bring sentient beings to full maturity. Lord of Secrets, apart from those who are unwilling, the lazy, those given to busyness, those who lack faith in mantra, and those who amass many possessions, consider how, for those who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva through the gateway of secret mantra, who possess full knowledge of the three pledges, and who accomplish the rites of secret mantra, there are no obstructors arising from conceptual thought. Drinking liquor and lying on a raised bed, these two are not to be done.

From The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment, the chapter on the practice of the three pledges.

How is one a Tathāgata?
How does one become the supreme Victor?
How is one a bodhisattva?
How is one called a buddha?
May the Great Sage
Dispel these doubts of mine.
Once doubt and hesitation are dispelled,
The bodhisattvas of great renown
Will practice the Great Vehicle,
The unsurpassed ocean of conduct.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, having looked upon the assembled retinue, complete in every respect, said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, what you have asked me is excellent, excellent! Therefore listen as I explain the way of the Great Vehicle.

Awakening has the characteristic of space;
It has abandoned all conceptualization.
Whoever wishes to realize it
Is called a bodhisattva.
One who has accomplished the ten grounds,
Has well realized and fully understood the ten masteries,
Knows in all their aspects phenomena
Empty and like illusions, and here
Knows the ways of the whole world,
Is called a buddha.
The Dharma has the characteristic of space;
It is nondual and of a single characteristic.
One fully awakened through the ten powers
Is called a perfectly complete buddha.
Because even mere perception is abandoned,
Its own essence cannot be shown.
What is called self-aware wisdom
Is explained to be the Tathāgata."

From The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment, the chapter on teaching the Tathāgata.

"Vajrapāṇi, furthermore, once, when I had become a bodhisattva and was practicing the conduct of a bodhisattva while dwelling in the world of Brahmā, Brahmā asked me, 'O Great Brahmā, how many kinds of fire should be known?' And I said this to him: 'The proud fire is the self-born one. After it comes the son of Brahmā, born from it.

The worldly fire Pāvaka
Is the first son of Brahmā.
His son is Brahmā's Food, and his son in turn
Is said to be the one called Bharata.
His son is Vaiśvānara,
Then Havana, and Oblation-Bearer;
Side-Guardian, it is renowned in the ocean,
Is the son of Atharvan.
The fire called Vāyu
Is taught for the rite of entering the womb.
Then for the bathing of a person
Know likewise Pavamāna.
The one called Auspicious is for the parting of the hair;
For the rite of birth, Pragalbha.
The naming fire is Pārthiva;
Likewise for the first feeding, Pure.
The one called Śadvi is for the topknot;
For conferring the vows, Samudbhava.
The one called the Sun is for the gift of cattle;
For taking a bride, Joiner is taught.
With Agni one kindles the household;
For offerings, Fire itself.
The fire called Pāvaka
Is taught to be applied to all the gods.
For the householder, Brahmā's fire;
Likewise for offering wealth, Peace.
Likewise for tethering cattle,
The fire called Avanika.
For transgressions, Vicici;
For cooked offerings, Sāhasa.
For the junctures of time, know Havi;
Likewise for the lunar stations, Treasure.
The one called Mṛṭa is for the final full oblation;
For pacifying, Dāruṇa is held to be right.
The fire at the end of rites is Yama;
Likewise for increase, Giver of Strength.
For all fierce activities
The wrathful fire is proclaimed.
The fire called Desire-Born
Is taught for the purpose of subjugation.
For burning forests, the Messenger fire;
The fire within the belly is Jaṭhara.
The one that devours is said to be the flesh-eater (piśāca);
The Mare's Mouth is in the ocean.
At the time of destruction it is called Terrifying,
And is rightly proclaimed the fire at the end of time.
These are the fires in summary,
Which I have explained to you;
Those who practice the heard scripture of the Vedas
Declare them to be the sons of Brahmā.'
These forty-four
I taught at that time.
Lord of Secrets, in this regard,
Those who perform fire offerings
Without knowing the nature of the fires
Do not make a fire offering, do no good deed,
And there will be no fruit of the action either.

After I had fully and perfectly awakened to enlightenment, I taught twelve fires. If you ask what they are:

The fire of wisdom is the first;
It is proclaimed to be Mahendra.
It is yellow in color
And bestows complexion, increase, and strength.
Garlanded with flames, it is samādhi,
And is said to bring wisdom to perfection.
The second is called Holder,
Like the light of the autumn moon;
Glorious, clad in white garments,
It holds a rosary, a water pot, and a ewer.
The third is called Vāyu;
Its color is black and it is rough.
The fourth is called Red,
Like the red glow of the evening twilight.
The fifth, called Mṛḍa,
Is russet in its coloring,
With a long neck and great splendor;
It is said to benefit all.
The sixth is called Wrathful,
Squint-eyed, colored like smoke,
Its hair bristling upward, it gives a great roar,
Four-fanged and of great strength.
The seventh is called Belly,
Sharp, and possessing every color.
The eighth is called Exhaustion,
Like the flickering of lightning rays.
The ninth is called Mind-Born,
Of various forms and of great strength.
The tenth is the World Holder.
The fire of the piśācas is another besides these.
The twelfth is called Stupefier,
For it stupefies all sentient beings.
Lord of Secrets, in this way,
Having consecrated the form of the fire,
Make your own form
And the substances too of its very nature.
This is the outer fire offering;
Those who wish for accomplishment should perform the fire offering.

Lord of Secrets, the inner one is of a single nature: when the three are present by way of making the three places one, that is the inner fire offering. Lord of Secrets, possessing great loving-kindness and great compassion is pacifying. Possessing joy is increasing. With the wrathful fire one should perform fierce activity: making wrath itself powerful, insert the name between ṭa and pha, place an effigy too in the wrathful maṇḍala, and do it by subduing anger. In the center place the fire of wind.

"Lord of Secrets, in this way, in each of the places just proclaimed, one should perform the fire offering as is appropriate and as is fitting."

Then again Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, how is the fire pit to be made?
How too are the purifying sprinklings to be done?
How is the kuśa grass to be spread?
Please tell me how it is to be contemplated."

When he had asked this, the Blessed One said this to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi:

"Lord of Secrets, as for the fire pit,
It measures about one cubit all around;
Its rim should measure four fingers,
And it is marked with vajra signs.
Starting from the right side of the pit,
Spread fresh kuśa grass;
Wrap the tips with the roots,
And the roots with the tips.
Here do the sprinkling from the right side,
In the same way as the kuśa is laid around.
Unguents, flowers, and incense
Should be offered to the Mṛḍa fire.
In that hearth the Mṛḍa fire,
With a single flower, as one's own form,
The disciplined mantra practitioner should establish.
Then too the sprinklings are done.
With his own secret mantra
The wise one should offer it the full oblation.
Then he should perform the pacifying fire offering,
Or else the increasing fire offering.
This worldly fire offering
Is declared to be the outer one.

Furthermore, the inner fire offering
Is the restraining of action and of life;
It is to be known by the mind,
Abandoning form, sound, and the rest.

The eye, ear, nose, tongue, and body are born from mentation and abide in the king of mind. These, produced by conceptualization, together with the awakening mind, the sense objects, the eye and so on, and the fire of wind as well, are restrained by unwavering wisdom, and one makes the fire offering into the fire of mind.

This is called the inner fire offering;
Gentle one, I have taught it to you."

Then the Blessed One said to the assembled retinue, complete in every respect: "You should practice this Dharma discourse while abiding in heedfulness.

Apart from my sons,
Do not give it to anyone else.
I shall explain their characteristics;
All of you, listen.
Born of the lineage of the Sun,
Proud, and of subtle intelligence,
Grateful, desiring the Dharma,
Knowing the Dharma and striving in the Dharma,
With a complexion of bluish hue,
With a very handsome face,
A high nose with a sharp tip,
And a broad head and forehead:
All these are my sons;
They reflect on the meaning of the teaching.
To them, earnestly,
This should always be given."

Then all of them, their minds overjoyed, said, "We shall do just as the Teacher has declared."

Then at that time all of them
Bowed to the Tathāgata,
Made offerings well,
And, seated before him with joined palms,
Spoke these words:
"So that this way of the Dharma
May long endure throughout the world,
Protector, please speak words of empowerment
For this teaching."

Then at that time the Blessed One spoke words empowering this Dharma discourse:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ sarbvathā śiṃ śiṃ traṃ traṃ guṃ guṃ dharaṃ dharaṃ sthāpaya buddhā satya vāka dharmma satya vāka saṃgha satya vāka hūṃ hūṃ veda vidi svāhā

When the Blessed One had said this, those vajra holders and the bodhisattvas Samantabhadra and the rest rejoiced and praised what the Blessed One had spoken.

From the great and most extensive king of sūtras, The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment, the twenty-fifth chapter, on all the rites for practicing the conduct of a bodhisattva through the gateway of secret mantra, is complete.

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said this: "For the benefit and happiness of those who are devoted to practicing in this way, I shall explain the bindings for them in this teaching. Gather your mind one-pointedly and listen.

Raising the left foot,
Plant the right one well and firmly.
Direct the gaze toward the waist.
Both hands should be raised.
The right, extended, stays in the manner of a serpent's hood;
Bind it so that it is joined with the wheel.
By properly forming the wheel mudrā,
One should crush in every direction.
Having seen this mudrā of great power,
Whether Vajradhara himself
Or whoever is worldly,
All of them will be destroyed.
Placing both feet evenly,
Thunder out the roar called hūṃ.
Fierce, pressing the lower lip with the teeth,
Wrathful, with the head raised,
If one threatens, they will be overpowered.
Putting both heels together,
If with the spear mudrā one strikes them all,
They instantly turn to ashes.
Quickly taking a single step,
Raising the foot and turning the body,
In the manner of a bow,
Having crushed the whole face of the earth on every side,
The gods, asuras, and yakṣas
Who dwell here upon the earth,
Whenever the mantra practitioner abides here,
He always makes the obstructors vanish.
Extending the left foot,
Join both hands like a lotus;
The mantra practitioner, blazing, circles the crown of the head,
And with the foot strikes out like a lion's stance.
If, with wrath, one strikes the obstructors,
Even if a buddha had made them,
Those obstructors are destroyed into ashes
Or without doubt will die.

Whatever accomplishments the wise
Have taught in all the tantras,
If all of them are summarized,
Are included without remainder in four kinds:
The first of the activities is pacifying,
And likewise the others, increasing,
Subjugating, and the activity of fierce conduct.
These four kinds are proclaimed.

Among them, accomplishments such as liberation, pacifying harms such as those of grahas, and lengthening life are pacifying. The wish-fulfilling jewel, becoming a sword-bearing vidyādhara, the kingdom of a universal monarch, and so on, and any accomplishing of wealth, all of these are increasing; so say those skilled in secret mantra. Bringing under control and summoning men, women, boys, girls, kings, ministers, and so on, as well as gods, nāgas, yakṣas, yakṣiṇīs, asura women, piśāca women, and so on, is bringing under one's control; so say the vidyā holders.

Binding, harming, cutting off limbs, setting at odds, causing enmity, killing, withering, and so on, in short, anything whatsoever that harms the bodies of sentient beings: all of these are fierce activity; so say the vidyā holders.

Through the actions of fire offering that satisfy,
The secret mantras swiftly grant accomplishment.
They empower all the gods,
And also bestow what is sublime.
Whatever is deficient, or not provided, or a lapse in the rite,
A letter crooked or lengthened, added or omitted,
Suppressed, or seized by obstructors,
All of that it purifies.
Without the fire offering, from the rites
Accomplishment will not come about.
For that very reason, one should apply oneself
To the definite actions of the fire offering.

The distinctions of the fire offering should be drawn according to the distinctions of pacifying, increasing, bringing under control, and fierce activity.

Among these, first, the definite fire offering for the activity of pacifying is as follows. Go about one krośa to the north of the village and examine the ground carefully. Whether on the bank of the river Gaṅgā, on the shore of a lotus lake, on the bank of a stream, or on an island in a lake, on ground that is white in color, empower yourself as Vajrasattva, perform the urging of the earth, its purification, and so on, and then begin the maṇḍala on the fifth, the fourth, or the tenth day of the waxing fortnight, or on the full moon; this is how it should be done. Uttarabhādrapadā, Pūrvaphalgunī, Āśleṣā, and Maghā are said to be the lunar mansions for pacifying. One should first begin the work when the planets the Moon and Venus hold sway.

Then, transforming yourself into the body of Avalokiteśvara, place the Blessed One Vairocana upon a lotus, as the sword mudrā or as the seed syllable, the letter a. a

Then to the north place the triangle mudrā of all the buddhas, or its seed syllable, on top of a lotus. aṃ

To the west of the buddhas place Ākāśagarbha, as the sword mudrā or as the seed syllable. ī

To the west of Ākāśagarbha is Mañjuśrī; place him too as the utpala mudrā or as the seed syllable. māṃ

To the west of him place Gaganalocanā, as the mudrā or as the seed syllable. gāṃ

To the east of the buddhas place Śākyamuni, as the alms bowl mudrā or as the seed syllable. bhā

Beside him place the wish-fulfilling jewel mudrā of the ūrṇā, or its seed syllable. bhru

Then draw the wish-fulfilling jewel mudrā of the bodhisattvas together with its seed syllable; the seed syllable for it is this: ka

Then on the right side of Great Vairocana place Avalokiteśvara, as the lotus mudrā or as the seed syllable. sa

On the left side of Avalokiteśvara place Mahāsthāmaprāpta, as the lotus mudrā or as the seed syllable. mā

Beside Mahāsthāmaprāpta place Bhṛkuṭī, as the water pot and ewer mudrā or as the seed syllable. bhṛ

To the right of the Lotus Holder place the goddess Tārā, as the utpala mudrā or as the seed syllable. tāṃ

Beside her place Pāṇḍaravāsinī, as the lotus mudrā or as the seed syllable. paṃ

On the left side of the Blessed One place Vajradhara, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. baṃ

On his right side place Māmakī, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. mā

Beside her place the vidyā queen Beautiful Destroyer, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. saṃ

On the left side of Vajradhara place Amṛtakuṇḍalin, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. mṛ

Beside him place all the vajra holders, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. hūṃ

Below the Blessed One, in the direction of Agni, place Acala, as the sword mudrā or as the seed syllable. hāṃ

Below the Blessed One, in the direction of Nairṛti, place Trailokyavijaya, as the vajra club mudrā or as the seed syllable. hā

At the two sides of the gate place the two nāga kings Nanda and Upananda, in the form of their names, "Nanda" and "Upananda."

Inside the gateways arrange the four guardians: in the east a sword with its seed syllable, ca; in the north a sword with its seed syllable, ba; in the west a club with its seed syllable, sa; in the south a club with its seed syllable, kṣa.

Then, inside the outer line, in the small maṇḍalas of the abodes, draw the five Pure Abodes. In the first maṇḍala it is saṃ saṃ svāhā. In the second it is manomaya. In the third it is dharmmā saṃbhava. In the fourth it is bibhava. In the fifth it is katanana. Alternatively, it is also permissible to place saṃ saṃ svāhā in the small maṇḍalas of the abodes.

In the place of the lord of the family to which one's own deity belongs, one should place one's own mudrā or seed syllable.

Then, inside the outer line, draw lotuses all around. Then, to the right of the gate of the maṇḍala, inside the surrounding ground, make the fire pit:

It is round all the way around,
And its measure should be about one cubit.
Its depth measures twelve fingers.
Above it, the rim
Should measure four fingers all around.
In the center of its floor
Make a wheel rim of one finger.

Then sit down, bind the pledge mudrā, and recite the pledge secret mantra. With the letter sa and the lotus mudrā, empower yourself as Avalokiteśvara. Imagine that from the letter a abiding in its own maṇḍala you become exactly the body of Great Vairocana, and display his mudrā as well. Then bind the sword mudrā, and from the letter a empower yourself as the body of Great Vairocana. Imagine that all the letters of the maṇḍala become the forms of their respective deities, and display each of their mudrās as well.

Then, reciting the mantra of noble Acala and binding the karma vajra, offer unguents, flowers, and so on to all the deities, however many reside in their respective retinues. Bringing to mind the samādhi that thinks, "Through the letter ra this too is free from dust," consecrate the unguents, flowers, and so on with their respective secret mantras; consecrate the unguents and the rest also with their respective heart mantras, and offer them. One should also invite each one individually, saying, "Accept this unguent and be mindful of me." Apply this likewise to all of them.

namaḥ samanta buddhāṇāṃ traṭa amogha caṇḍa mahāroṣaṇa sphoṭaya hūṃ traṭa haṃ ma
This is the secret mantra of noble Acala.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ viśuddhe gandhodbhava svāhā
This is the secret mantra of unguent.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ mahā maitrya bhyuṅgate svāhā
This is the secret mantra of flowers.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ dharma dhātvā nugate svāhā
This is the secret mantra of incense.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ tathāgatārci spharaṇa avabhāsa gaganodarya svāhā
This is the secret mantra of lamps.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ traiyadhvikānāṃ arara parara balide baliṃ dhadāhi svāhā
This is the secret mantra of food offerings.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ gagana sama sama svāhā
This is the secret mantra of the water offering; it is applied at the beginning and at the end as well.

The rite of reciting the heart secret mantra is:
oṃ bhagavān vairocana idaṃ gaṃdhi pratigṛhya idaṃ me kāryaṃ kuru svāhā
Apply this likewise to all of them.

For the pacifying rite, offer white sandalwood mixed with camphor; if that is not available, offer puśelce. Likewise offer white lotus flowers, jasmine flowers, mallikā flowers, yūthikā flowers, and such fragrant white flowers as are auspicious. For incense in the pacifying rite, offer camphor and sandalwood, or else śivasaka. As food offerings, offer white things such as yogurt and cooked rice. As for lamps, offer butter lamps poured into white vessels. Offer a water offering in white vessels such as conch shells, filled with water mixed with sandalwood, camphor, and white flowers.

Having made the offerings as described, then go outside, empower yourself as Avalokiteśvara, and give the torma to the directions outside:

With parched rice and with sesame,
With water and with cooked rice,
With flowers and with cooked beans,
With kṛśara, and with plenty of oil and fat:
Such a torma the master
Should take up in his hand and, joyfully,
Scatter in all directions,
Offering incense and water as well.
First, in the direction of Īśāna,
Offer to the Bow-Bearer (Rudra) with his host.
Then in the direction of Indra
The wise one should offer to Indra himself.
Then the fearless mantra practitioner
Should offer in the direction of Agni.
Then the dauntless mantra practitioner
Should offer in the direction of Yama.
The knower of mantra, in the direction of Nairṛti,
Offers to Nairṛti with his host.
In the direction of Varuṇa, to the nāgas,
He offers while reciting the fearless secret mantra.
Having offered to them together with their lord,
He should go to the direction of Vāyu.
The knower of mantra, having gone there,
To Vāyu, the wind god, with his host,
Calling them by their own names,
Offers torma with their own mantras.
Having gone to the northern direction,
With his mind made fully clear,
Wishing to attain his own accomplishment,
He offers torma to the yakṣas.
To the rākṣasas, the piśācas,
The bhūtas who eat raw flesh,
And to sentient beings of every kind,
The wise one scatters torma in the directions.
To the beings of that place,
Who dwell in trees and in thickets,
Whoever comes to mind,
To each of them he gives torma.

The mantras of the torma for the directions are these. In the direction of Īśāna: rudrāya svāhā. In the direction of Indra: śakrāya svāhā. In the direction of Agni: agnaye svāhā. In the direction of Yama: vaiva svāhā. In the direction of Nairṛti: rākṣasādhipataye svāhā. In the direction of Varuṇa: meghaśanaye svāhā. In the direction of Vāyu: vāyave svāhā. In the direction of Kubera: yakśa vidyādhari svāhā. kraṃ kiri svāhā: this is for the rākṣasas. mici mici svāhā: this is for the piśācas. gu i gu i svāhā: this is for the bhūtas.

Having thus scattered torma all around, wash your hands and rinse your mouth. Then go inside, bow to all the buddhas, purify with noble Acala whatever substances there are to be burned, and place them on the right side. On the left side place two vessels for the water offering, filled with kuśa grass and water mixed with fragrances. One is recited over with noble Acala; the other is recited over with the Supreme Vajra Giver. Spread kuśa grass around the fire hearth in an encircling manner. On the seat where you yourself sit, also lay out kuśa grass in an encircling manner. Place in front of you the filling ladle and the pouring ladle of udumbara or aśvattha wood, and melted butter as well.

Then smear the fire pit with sandalwood and scatter loose fragrant white flowers. Take fire from the house of a brahmin, sprinkle it with the king of the wrathful ones, and kindle the fire on firewood of plakṣa and udumbara with a fan. Sit there facing north, anoint both hands with unguent, bind the pledge mudrā and the others, and with mudrā and secret mantra empower yourself as the body of Great Vairocana, exactly as he abides in the water maṇḍala.

Then imagine a water maṇḍala at the floor of the fire pit. In the center of the water, from the letter placed as before, it becomes the form of fire. Imagine the fire god thus: like a conch, a kunda flower, and the Moon in color, with his hair in a topknot, three-eyed and four-armed, holding in his hands a rosary, a water pot and ewer, and a club, and granting fearlessness; wearing white garments, with a white thread worn across his body as a sacred cord, marked at the heart with a triangle, marked with the letter ra, radiating white flames, and having the nature of wisdom.

With the water arranged earlier and consecrated by noble Acala, sprinkle around the fire in a circling manner. Do it three times with the Supreme Vajra Giver as well.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ maṃ svāhā
This is the Supreme Vajra Giver's.

Then offer unguent, flowers, and so on to the fire with its own secret mantra. Then give three burnt oblations of the three sweet substances. Holding the filling ladle in your fist, give three full ladles of butter. Then burn seven pairs of kindling sticks from the tips of udumbara or aśvattha, dipped in butter. Then again give three full ladles of butter, and do the sprinkling as before.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ oṃ vradāganaye śāntiṃ kuru svāhā

In the same way, perform the fire offering to each of the deities individually, and do the sprinkling.

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ hūṃ a mahā śānti gata śāntikara praśama dharmma nirjāta abhāva svabhūva dharmma samanta prāpta amukasya svasti śāntiṃ baṃ kuru svāhā This is the secret mantra of pacifying.

Then, bringing to mind all the buddhas, meditate on the awakening mind just as it is, through the purity of the inner self. Then place your own vidyā-mantra on a moon maṇḍala, bind the mudrā of your own deity, and meditate on yourself in the form of your own deity. Then again, as before, offer the three full ladles and the rest, do the sprinkling in an encircling manner, and perform the fire offering with the substances arising from your own tantra for as long as you do not tire. When you grow weary, gaze upon the body of the perfect Buddha and meditate once more on the awakening mind. Then offer the water offering, bow to the deities, and request them to depart. Having requested all the deities to depart, cover the assemblies of mudrās with new cloth. On the following day, as before, imagine the deities abiding in their respective maṇḍalas, and carry out that same sequence of the rite until pacification has come about.

The accomplished mantra practitioner who knows the rite,
Whoever pacifies by means of this rite
Without laziness,
Will attain pacification itself.
In half a month a single realm
Will certainly be pacified;
For a king, in twelve days;
For a region, in ten days.

In this regard, one who performs the activity of the pacifying rite should practice the rite as follows:

Wearing white clothes, clean and peaceful,
Wholly devoted to the awakening mind,
He sleeps on a seat spread with kuśa grass,
And holds a purifier made of it.
He drinks barley gruel or milk,
And sets his mind on performing the fire offering.
With fire offering and recitation at the three times
He should practice the pacifying rite.
Water for the feet is offered with the Supreme Vajra Giver,
The sprinkling with Acala, the protector.
Here, for now, in summary,
The rite of fire offering is proclaimed.

From The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment, the chapter on the rite of the pacifying fire offering.

Then furthermore, O greatly fortunate one,
What the skilled in mantra formerly,
Out of loving concern for sentient beings,
Wishing to benefit them, taught
About the great increasing fire offering,
Listen to that with mind gathered one-pointedly.
Therefore in every way
One should practice the increasing rite.
Having made one's place in the eastern quarter
Of a village or of a district,
One should, as before, carefully examine
The ground that has been determined.
On a mountain peak, in a cattle pen,
Or at stūpas containing relics,
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala
For accomplishing the activity of increasing.
The skilled one, by the rite of the activity,
Having dug the ground well,
Places in the center precious things and grains
Mixed together with medicines.
There, with yellow earth,
One should fill the ground well.
Having made it level and well pounded,
On top of it one draws the maṇḍala.

Here too, for now, the mantra practitioner skilled in the rite meditates on himself as the Supreme Vajra Giver and, abiding in his own maṇḍala, empowers himself with its secret mantra and mudrā; with noble Acala he should seal the boundaries and take full possession of the ground. On the third, fifth, or seventh day of the waxing fortnight, or else on the full moon, under a good planet, Mercury or Jupiter, and under the lunar mansion Śravaṇa, Śatabhiṣaj, Jyeṣṭhā, or Rohiṇī, he empowers himself as Great Vairocana, takes a yellow cord, and strikes the lines of the maṇḍala with the same number and measurements as before. Then he circumambulates the maṇḍala, enters inside, recites the Blessed Mother, the Perfection of Wisdom, and bows. Again he empowers himself as Vairocana, takes yellow pigment, and

There places the letter ka.
At that moment the sage Kanakamuni,
Blazing with the color of gold,
Abides in samādhi and has destroyed evil.

Then draw the maṇḍala with its porticoes, and always open the western gate. Surround it on the outside with three square lines, and arrange the deities in the maṇḍala as before. In this there is the following distinction. In the place of noble Ākāśagarbha, place the mudrā of Samantabhadra, the jewel banner, together with the letter sa. sa

In the place of the ūrṇā, place noble Jambhala, as the mudrā of the bījapūra fruit together with the letter jaṃ, which has the character of nectar. jaṃ

In the place of noble Bhṛkuṭī, place Yaśovatī, as the mudrā of the priyaṅgu flower together with the letter ya. ya

In the place of noble Pāṇḍaravāsinī, place Mahāśrī, called the Noble White Lady, as the lotus mudrā or as the seed syllable. śrī

In the place of noble Beautiful Destroyer, place Subāhu, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. hūṃ

In the place of Amṛtakuṇḍalin, place Vajrasena, as the vajra mudrā or as the seed syllable. sa

In the place of the inhabitants of the Pure Abodes, arrange Śakra and the four world protectors.

Then, inside the two outer lines, draw vajras all the way around and in all the corners. Then, on the right side of the maṇḍala, inside the surrounding ground, make the fire pit:

Perfectly square, with a rim,
Its width should measure two cubits.
Encircle it all around with vajras.
Its depth measures about one cubit;
The rim measures one span to the little finger.
Plaster it with a wash of yellow clay.
The lotus mudrā measures one finger;
Make its height four fingers.

At the three times, smear it with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground. Then, as before, imagine the lord deity of the maṇḍala and the others exactly in their aspects and signs, and worship them with unguents, flowers, and so on. In this there is the following distinction: when one requests the Blessed One Vairocana to take his seat and makes offerings with unguents, flowers, and so on, at that time one should do so having empowered oneself as the Supreme Vajra Giver.

Here I shall explain the particulars of the unguents, flowers, and so on.

Saffron mixed with camphor
Is the finest unguent for rites of increase.
If that is not found, one should prepare
Something fragrant and yellow in color.
Campaka flowers are most excellent;
Yūthikā, yellow jasmine,
And others too that are fragrant
And yellow should be offered.
Saffron blended with aloeswood,
And blended with sugar,
In order to accomplish the rites of increase,
The wise one should offer as incense.
Guggulu and sandalwood too,
Blended with ghee, are the finest incense.
Sarja resin mixed with saffron
The one skilled in mantra should offer as incense.
A lamp vessel of gold is best;
Or else a yellow one of clay will also do.
The wise one should offer a yellow lamp wick
Made of yellow cloth.
With fine scented oil
Its oil should be made fragrant;
Or else, with sesame oil,
One should offer a clean lamp.
Yellow rice with a lentil dish,
Together with ghee, is most excellent.
The side dishes too should be made yellow,
And likewise the fruits and pastries.
Scented water with yellow flowers,
And likewise rice and dūrvā grass,
Poured into a vessel that is also yellow,
Is the auspicious guest-water for rites of increase.

Having performed the offerings properly in this way, one should then empower oneself as the Supreme Vajra Giver, go outside, and give the torma to the directions. Then, having performed the ritual sipping, one should go inside, pay homage to all the buddhas, and, as before, set down beside the fire pit the things to be burned and so on, purified with mantra. Sitting in the lotus posture upon kuśa grass spread on the ground, one should imagine oneself in the body of Great Vairocana. Smear the room and the hearth with yellow unguent, scatter loose yellow flowers, and spread kuśa grass. Take fire from a household of the warrior caste or from a royal palace, perform the purifying sprinkling, and kindle the fire with plakṣa and udumbara wood.

Then imagine the maṇḍala of Mahendra at the base of the fire pit, and imagine there the syllable ya with an anusvāra, resting upon a lotus: yaṃ. From the syllable ra, one should imagine fire arising from within the fire pit, as follows: he has a topknot the color of gold, three eyes, and four faces; in his hands he holds a rosary and a water pot, and makes the gesture of granting the supreme; he wears yellow garments and a yellow thread across his shoulder as a sacred cord; he is smeared with three lines and bears the mark of a triangle; he is marked with the syllable ra; he is like gold in color, with blazing flames radiating in every direction, and possesses great power. Having imagined such a fire, arisen from the light rays of the king of secret mantra, one should then perform the purifying sprinkling, the rinsing of the mouth, and the offering of guest-water, and give unguents, flowers, and so on with his own mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānān oṃ agnaye svāhā

Then give three oblations of milk porridge blended with honey, ghee, and so on, or of black sesame blended with ghee. Then, with a filling ladle and a pouring ladle of bilva wood, give seven ladlefuls of ghee. Then give three oblations of kindling sticks taken from the middle part of plakṣa or udumbara wood, a thumb in thickness and a cubit in length, dipped in ghee, and again give three ladlefuls. Then, imagining the forms of the deities as before, offer three oblations with the heart mantra of each. Then, imagining all the buddhas, cultivate the awakening mind, imagine the form of one's own deity, and, having offered three oblations as before, perform the fire offering with the substances described in the accomplishment of one's own siddhi. Saying oṃ at the beginning, add at the end: "For fame, glory, wealth, virtue, and riches, svāhā." Or else, saying, "For the substance of the accomplishment I have conceived, svāhā," one should offer the oblations. Having performed the fire offering properly, one should carry out the same sequence of the awakening mind and so on, as before.

Here too, the rite to be practiced by the practitioner is this:

He should wear yellow garments
And carry a clean, yellow purifier.
His mind steady and his power great,
Keeping strict silence and free of avarice,
Always delighting in the awakening mind,
First performing the rite at the three times,
Eating cooked barley or drinking milk,
He should undertake the rite of increase.
Whoever, by this means, properly
Undertakes the rite of increase,
Whatever increase he has in mind
Will swiftly be accomplished.
A sword, a wheel, a vajra,
Realgar, eye ointment, bezoar,
A wish-fulfilling jewel, a vase, a garment,
A falling rain of riches, sandals,
A parasol, a chariot, a mount,
And likewise a multistoried house,
A lotus, a vase, a trident,
A rosary, a club, a gundhe,
A lute, a short spear, or an arrow,
An alms bowl and a ringing staff,
Or else whatever substance one desires
Should be accomplished in this maṇḍala.

This concludes the chapter on the ritual of the fire offering for the rite of increase, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then, furthermore, this fire offering
That fully accomplishes the rite of control
Was taught by the Lamp of the World
With a mind that had turned to love.
I shall explain it without omission;
You who desire prosperity, listen to me.
First, as for examining the site:
Make your dwelling to the southeast, the direction of Agni,
Of a town or else of a region,
In an empty house where no people are,
Or at the foot of a solitary tree, or in a charnel ground,
Or else at a shrine of Śaṃkara (Śiva),
Or at a shrine of the mātṛkās; a cave will also do.
As for the mantrin's drawing of the maṇḍala,
On the ninth day of the waning fortnight
One should begin the rite of control.
On the day of the Sun or of Mars,
Under the lunar mansions Puṣya, Kṛttikā,
Maghā, and Viśākhā,
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala.

For this, the mantrin first empowers himself as Vajrapāṇi, and on ground of a red color performs the purification of the ground, the taking possession of it, and so on. Then, having empowered himself as Great Vairocana, he takes a thread dyed crimson over which the noble Acala's mantra has been recited, and lays out the lines of the maṇḍala: it is triangular, with its door facing east, and the door frame, the portico, and the border are properly made.

Then, taking red pigment
Of very fine quality,
He places the blazing syllable:
The syllable ra with its anusvāra,
Like the rising sun,
Very hard to tame, supreme among the victors.
Having empowered the pigment in this way
And having recollected all the buddhas,
He draws the maṇḍala all around:
Three cubits, or else five,
Or else seven or nine will also do.
In keeping with the siddhi of secret mantra,
He should divide it into three parts.
The placing of the deities is as before;
The wise one should do it with the lord of the family,
And the rest, as is fitting,
The wise one should place.
