There, in the place of Ākāśagarbha, one places the two, Keśinī and Upakeśinī, with the mudrās of the club and the noose. In the place of the Beautiful Destroyer one places Vajra Hook with the mudrā of the arrow. Crown Conduct too is placed with the mudrā of the arrow. Outside, one draws the four servants with the mudrā of the noose. Then one performs all the rites as before. The unguents, flowers, and so on that are offered are likewise red.

The fire pit, too, is made in the same place as before. It should measure twenty fingers in length, with a rim of two fingers, its opening facing outward. Its depth is one small span, and in the center one should make an eight-finger vajra smeared with red. That fire pit should be triangular, with three corners. Then, having empowered oneself as Vajrapāṇi, one gives the torma outside.

Then take fire from the house of a dyer and perform the purifying sprinkling as before. Kindle the fire with khadira wood, aśoka wood, or karavīra wood. Likewise, having empowered oneself as Vairocana, imagine the maṇḍala of fire at the base of the fire pit and place the syllable there. One imagines that this syllable ra, through light radiating from a garland of flames, turns into the form of Agni: like the rising sun, hard to tame, three-eyed, with a tawny topknot and four arms, holding in his hands a club, a noose, a rosary, and a gundhe; he wears red garments and bears a red thread across his shoulder, is marked with the syllable ra, and radiates red light with blazing flames. Having imagined such a fire, perform the purifying sprinkling, offer guest-water, and give unguents, flowers, and so on with his own secret mantra.

Then give seven oblations of kindling sticks of karavīra wood dipped in mustard oil and red sandalwood. Then offer seven oblations to the deities arranged in the maṇḍala. As before, imagine the form of one's own deity and set it in place, likewise offer oblations, and perform the fire offering with the substances that come from the rite. At the end too, perform in the same way the rite of cultivating the awakening mind and so on. In this rite, too, all the requisites of the maṇḍala should be made red. The place of the fire, too, should be smeared with red sandalwood at the three times, and loose red flowers should be scattered over it.

He wears glorious red garments,
Is adorned with red unguent and garlands,
And bears a red thread across his shoulder;
He knows the distinctions of the awakening mind.
For food he eats whatever small beans there are,
Or else for food he eats fruit.
Joyful and smiling, able to keep silence,
He joins mind with mind,
And constantly performs recitation and fire offering.
The person who contemplates the form of the deity
Should undertake every accomplishment
Of bringing under control.
Whoever, by this rite,
Knowing the ritual, undertakes the rite,
A majestic one or his son
Will come under his control within one month.
If he performs the fire offering for two months,
Male and female servants, yakṣiṇīs,
And likewise the maidens of the underworld
The mantrin will swiftly summon.
This, for now, in brief,
Is the proclamation of the fire offering for control.

This concludes the chapter on the ritual of the fire offering for bringing under control, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Now I shall explain without omission
The rite of fierce conduct.
Yakṣas, mahoragas, cruel pretas,
Asuras, gandharvas, kiṃnaras,
Beings who delight in harm and are hostile,
Likewise obstructors and vināyakas,
Bhūtas, mātṛkās, rākṣasas,
Ḍākinīs and pūtanas,
And even humans who are arrogant, proud, and powerful,
Who do violent deeds or are hostile:
In order to tame them, the one who knows mantra
Should undertake the fierce rite.
Against minor offenses
The wrathful rite is not to be undertaken.

To the south of a town,
The one who strives in his vows, on black ground,
In a solitary charnel ground or a hermitage,
Or in an old pleasure grove, a peaceful place,
On the eighth day of the waning fortnight,
Or else on the fourteenth day,
On the day of Saturn or of Mars,
Under the lunar mansions that arise from wind,
Hasta and Citrā,
Aśvinī and likewise Uttaraphalgunī,
Punarvasu and Svāti:
These lunar mansions are fierce,
And on any one of them
The mantrin, wrathful like Yama,
Should draw the terrifying maṇḍala
That cuts off the life of the enemy.
With his own seal the mantrin
Empowers himself as Vajrasattva,
And, fearless in mantra,
Purifies the ground as before.
Then, with Acala of great power,
Having performed the taking possession and so on,
With the mudrā of his own syllable
He then makes himself Vairocana.
Taking there a blue thread
Spun by a young maiden,
He carefully lays the lines of the maṇḍala:
It is made triangular, and round on the outside.
Four cubits, five cubits, or eight cubits,
Or likewise twelve or sixteen cubits:
In keeping with the rite,
The wise one should determine its measure.
Then, with ash from a charnel ground,
Or with black pigment, the wise one
Places there the syllable ha,
Radiating a garland of blazing flames,
A form terrifying even to the fire at the end of time,
The color of a summer rain cloud,
Its mouth baring fangs:
Vajrapāṇi, wearing a diadem,
Who threatens all the hosts of Māra
And all who are hostile.
The one skilled in pigments, having placed
Such a syllable in those pigments,
The one endowed with wisdom draws the maṇḍala,
Marked with the sign of many banners,
With portico and door frame,
And the portions of the doors well divided.
The placing of the chief deity
Should be determined as before.
Having opened the northern door,
One should close the remaining doors.

Then, in the place of the noble Ākāśagarbha, draw the king of the wrathful ones, Yamāri, with the mudrā of the stake. In the place of the ūrṇā, draw the king of the wrathful ones, the noble one called Aparājita, with the mudrās of the vajra and the axe. In the places of Mahāsthāmaprāpta, the noble Bhṛkuṭī, and the noble Tārā, place Amoghapāśa, Ekajaṭī, and Hayagrīva, in that order, with the mudrās of the noose, the axe, and the vajra club. In the place of the Beautiful Destroyer, place the Chief Wrathful Moon with the vajra mudrā. The noble Acala, too, is placed with the mudrā of the club. Beside the doors, place the two, Durdharṣa and Abhimukha, with the mudrā of the club. In the place of the gods of the Pure Abodes, place Yama's Mṛtyu, the lord of death, together with Kālarātri. Nairṛti, too, is placed with the mudrā of the sword. Within the area for circumambulation, the fire pit should be made facing the door. Outside the maṇḍala, all around its circumference, draw banners.

The fire pit is made like the fire pit for the rite of control, with this distinction, that its outer rim is made round and marked with many banners. For the deities of the maṇḍala, imagine their individual forms as before and perform the rite of worship. Then, having worshiped the deities properly, empower oneself as Vajrapāṇi and give the torma of the directions outside, as before.

Here, the particulars of the unguents and so on are these. Offer a black unguent of red sandalwood mixed with turuṣka and charcoal. Offer flowers: blue utpala, blue aparājita, or other blue flowers. As incense, offer sarja resin blended with molasses. As the lamp, offer pungent mustard oil with a blue wick in a black lamp vessel. As the food offering, offer kṛsara and other foods that are pungent, bitter, and so on, together with side dishes of black color. Lentil rice, kṛsara, and meat and fish, cooked and raw, are given as the torma of the directions.

Having performed the torma rite properly, take fire from the house of a caṇḍāla or from a charnel ground, perform the purifying sprinkling with the king of the wrathful ones, and kindle the fire with wood from a charnel ground, or with bhallātaka wood, or with bibhītaka wood. Having empowered oneself as Great Vairocana, imagine the maṇḍala of wind at the base of the fire pit and place there the syllable hūṃ. One imagines that from that syllable hūṃ the form of the fire arises: black in color and exceedingly fierce, three-eyed, four-faced, unmoving and smoke-colored. He is hard to behold, tawny, with a smoke-colored topknot and light rays like a blue rain cloud. He is big-bellied, wears blue garments, and bears a blue thread across his shoulder. Four fangs are bared in his mouth; he is wrathful, with his mouth gaping open. In his hands he holds a sword, a noose, a club, and a water pot. His heart bears the mark of a triangle, and he is marked with the syllable ra. His face is wrinkled in a frown. Together with the sound hūṃ, he blazes, black and rough, with a garland of radiating light rays like copper, and he abides as though the time of destruction had arrived. Having imagined such a fire, perform the purifying sprinkling and give unguents and so on with his own vidyā-mantra:

oṃ agnaye jāhi trad ṭa hūṃ phaṭ

Then give three oblations of kṛsara blended with pungent mustard oil, and give seven oblations of poison, blood, and mustard seed. Or else give kindling sticks of bhallātaka and bibhītaka wood, split with sharp points and dipped in pungent mustard oil and mustard seed. Then, imagining the lord of the maṇḍala and the other deities as before, offer oblations of those same substances with their own vidyās. Then imagine one's own deity as before and set it in place. Holding the substances that arise from one's own vidyā properly between the index finger and the thumb, recite the secret mantra wrathfully, and, adding phaṭ, offer oblations for as long as one wishes. Then, as before, one should rest by cultivating the awakening mind and so on.

Whatever mantrin performs
Such extremely fierce rites
Burns even the abode
Of the Thirty-Three like the finest grass.

Here too the rite should be performed in this way:

He wears glorious blue garments,
Is adorned with blue unguent and garlands,
And bears a blue thread across his shoulder;
He is always intent on the awakening mind.
He eats pungent, bitter, and astringent things,
And the food of kṛsara.
Skilled in the matter of arranging the deities,
Pure and supremely endowed with moral discipline,
Of great enthusiasm and with a courageous heart,
Wrathful like Antaka, the ender of the world,
Facing south and keeping silence,
As for his posture, he sits squatting.
Always diligent in fire offering and recitation,
Skilled in guarding and protecting,
Performing the fire offering at night,
He should undertake fierce conduct.

This concludes the chapter on the ritual of the fire offering of fierce conduct, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then once again the Blessed One Vairocana taught, to those who practice the conduct of bodhisattvas, the Dharma of the essential nature of the dharmadhātu: the domain of nondual emptiness, the cause of attaining nonconceptual wisdom, which is the heart of dependent origination; free of all elaboration, clear and pure, accomplished by the mind, completely perfect, and bringing to completion.

Then, at that time, the bodhisattva, the great being of great compassion, Avalokiteśvara, entered into equipoise in the samādhi of omniscient wisdom and said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, how will those who follow it, abiding in the way of mantra, attain that which is the domain of omniscient wisdom, which accords with emptiness, grants the siddhis of all secret mantras, is without location, without characteristics, and without essential nature, is free of both the basis of characterization and the characteristic, brings about the attainment of the Great Vehicle, and bestows the nectar of immortality?"

When he had said this, the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, who reveals the secrets of all the tathāgatas, looked at the face of the Blessed One Vairocana and said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, since the bodhisattva of great compassion, Avalokiteśvara, who looks upon the mind streams of all sentient beings, is asking me this, Blessed One, please teach the Dharma that benefits beings and brings about the attainment of the Great Vehicle; the domain that is without characteristics and nondual; that grants the siddhi of the way of all secret mantras; the essential nature of the heart of dependent origination, which accords with the dharmadhātu and gives rise to the world systems of the ten directions; the Dharma of the domain whose essential nature is emptiness."

When this had been requested, the Blessed One Vairocana, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, who brings the three vehicles to completion, who causes omniscient wisdom to be attained, who frees from the ocean of all conceptual thought, who has all sentient beings in mind, who grants the siddhis of all secret mantras, who terrifies all obstructors, who summons all bhūtas, the inconceivable heart of all bodhisattvas, without form, without sound, without smell, without taste, without touch, without location, without mentation, without consciousness, without letters, without domain, inexpressible, without elaboration, the unmingled one, spoke thus to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi:

"Of whatever dharmas arise from causes, their cause,
And also their cessation,
The Tathāgata has taught;
Thus the great śramaṇa has spoken.
Because the dependent does not exist,
The perfected too becomes otherwise.
The two do not exist;
The eight consciousnesses characterize the two."

Then all those of the great assembly who had gathered there, through the power of the miraculous display brought forth by their individual wisdom, understood the meaning of the two verses that had been spoken, each in keeping with their different intentions and different understandings. Those bodhisattvas then said to the Blessed One, "Excellent, excellent!" and offered these well-spoken words:

"Those who abide well in the way of secret mantra
And meditate on this very reality
Praised by the perfect buddhas
Will become buddhas themselves."

Then, at that time, the bodhisattva, the great being Avalokiteśvara, through the empowerment of the Blessed One Vairocana, said this to the Blessed One:

"If even through the unmingled mind itself,
Which has passed beyond recitation and conceptual thought,
Siddhi is attained,
What need is there to speak of conditioned things?
Whoever, having tamed through meditative equipoise
The mind that is like a horse,
Makes love and compassion firm,
Immediately attains stable siddhi.
Dharmas are forever unborn;
Their seed is the supreme a,
So the holy victors have taught:
a is called emptiness.
Since it is the mother of letters, the mother of all,
And likewise is endowed with the power to lead,
Unchanging and endowed with the unchanging,
Therefore it should be contemplated.
a and u,
ṛ, ḷ, and aṃ appear as supreme;
a is the holiest of the supreme appearances;
From it, it is taught, liberation is attained.
The ka class and the ca class,
Likewise the supreme ṭa class,
The ta class and the pa class,
These five together with their corresponding sounds,
And ya ra la va śa and the rest:
All are supreme victors, perfectly complete.
These are supreme in their vast meaning
And accomplish aims of many kinds.
By saying a, awakening is attained;
Likewise i is for summoning;
By saying u, killing is accomplished;
ṛ is for the rite of control;
o is the destroyer of rākṣasas;
Saying ai is held to be for binding;
au is the heart of the nāgas;
All of them are supreme and holy.
All the leading letters,
Endowed with ra and with the visarga,
When constantly joined with the dharmadhātu,
Through meditation and recitation bring the attainment of siddhi."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana said to the bodhisattva, the great being Avalokiteśvara:

"You have understood the realization of the ten grounds.
Great being, excellent, excellent!
You have explained the whole of the reality
Of the sugatas, perfect and inexhaustible.
You have realized the profound secret
Spoken by Vajrapāṇi."

Thus he gave his approval, saying, "Excellent!"

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said this to the Blessed One:

O hero, supreme victor, who turns
The wheel whose nature is the dharmadhātu!
Well restrained by the awakening mind,
With the mudrā of the hero of the world
And with the letter of nonarising,
Having likewise imagined oneself,
Through union with nondual emptiness
One should accomplish all the rites.
Having imagined the self, which is the completely pure dharmadhātu,
As endowed with the seed letter,
If one recites in equipoise and with faith,
One will attain siddhi.
Through bathing, confession, and so on,
Devotion to complete solitude,
And recitation that contemplates the mudrā and the garland of letters,
One attains stable siddhi.
If one practices with these mantras
That have become endowed with the dharmadhātu,
In the three realms of the moving and the unmoving
There is nothing that will not be accomplished.

Lord of Secrets, therefore, the one who performs recitation should establish the turning of the wheel in that rite of empowerment in his own maṇḍala.

This concludes the chapter on the arrangement of the letters for meditation and on empowerment, from The Manifest Awakening of Great Vairocana, His Transformations, and His Empowerment.

Then Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, please explain to the bodhisattvas, through various means and in keeping with their intentions and inclinations, the inconceivable dharmadhātu, the manifest and perfect awakening that comes about through the mind's perfectly passing beyond action. Sugata, please explain it."

Here, first of all, one should confess one's misdeeds individually with this rite: "All buddhas and bodhisattvas, please consider me. I, who am named so-and-so, from this time onward until I reach the heart of awakening, confess individually, in the presence of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas, all the misdeeds and nonvirtuous actions that I have committed, that I have caused others to commit, and in which I have rejoiced, while wandering in saṃsāra, in which births have no beginning and no end." This is done a second time, and likewise a third time.

Then, in the presence of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas, one should take refuge in the three: "All buddhas and bodhisattvas, please consider me. I, who am named so-and-so, from this time onward until I reach the heart of awakening, go for refuge to the buddhas, the blessed ones. I go for refuge to the dharmakāya of the tathāgatas, which is peaceful, unsurpassed, and to be known individually by oneself. I go for refuge to the Saṅgha of the great bodhisattvas of the four directions, the noble ones who are irreversible." This is done a second time, and likewise a third time.

Then, as for offering oneself: "All buddhas and bodhisattvas, please consider me. I, who am named so-and-so, from this time onward until I reach the heart of awakening, offer myself completely at all times to all the buddhas and bodhisattvas. Out of love for me, may all the buddhas and bodhisattvas please accept me." This is done a second time, and likewise a third time.

Then one should bind the samaya mudrā and recite the secret mantra:

namaḥ smanta buddhānān asame trisame samaye svāhā

Then one empowers oneself as the dharmadhātu:

namaḥ smanta buddhānān dharmmadhātu svabhāva ātmako 'haṃ

Then one empowers oneself as Vajrasattva:

namaḥ smanta buddhānān vajra ātmako 'haṃ

Then one should generate the mind set on awakening: "All buddhas and bodhisattvas, please consider me. I, who am named so-and-so, from this time onward until I reach the heart of awakening, generate the mind set on awakening just as the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattvas generated it: free of all entities; rid of the aggregates, the elements, the sense sources, the apprehended and the apprehender; one's own mind, because of the equality of the selflessness of dharmas, unborn from the very beginning, in the essential nature of emptiness. In the same way I too generate it." This is done a second time and a third time.

Then, binding the mudrā of fullness at the crown of the head and imagining clouds of incense, flowers, and so on throughout all the assemblies of the tathāgatas, one should recite this queen of vidyā-mantras three times:

namaḥ smanta buddhānāṃ abhismaye spharaṇa himāṃ gagan khaṃ dharmma dhātvā kāśa samatāya sarbba tathāgata pariṣadā maṇḍale mama praṇidhi puṇya jñāna balena sarbba tathāgata balena dharma dhātva dhiṣṭha na balena ca svāhā

The mudrā of fullness is that which is formed at the crown of the head like a lotus in bloom; that is the defining characteristic of the mudrā of fullness.

This concludes the chapter on the ritual of recitation.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, having shown the inconceivable display of the dharmadhātu, and wishing once again to show the inconceivable domain, caused to emerge from the uṣṇīṣa on the crown of his head a net of light rays called Treasury of Dharma, which illuminates the dense thicket of the minds of the infinite realms of sentient beings. That array of the net of great light rays, through the vast display of the empowerment of the tathāgatas, circled around the tathāgatas who dwell in the palace of the dharmadhātu, vast as the element of space. It illuminated all the maṇḍalas of the assemblies of all sentient beings and set itself to protecting completely the realms of sentient beings without exception. It satisfied even the beings born in the destinies of the hells, of animals, and of pretas, and empowered the worlds of gods and humans as well to belong within the Dense Array of the Tathāgatagarbha. Then he empowered the inconceivable dharmadhātu too through the inconceivable concordant cause of the empowerment of the power of the tathāgatas' merit, and, since the purpose of the dharmadhātu had not yet been fulfilled, he entered into equipoise in the samādhi of the great king of maṇḍalas called Treasury from Which the Heart of Great Compassion Arises, the heart that completely protects the undetermined realms of sentient beings.

Having entered into that equipoise, he addressed Vajrapāṇi and the other vajra holders, as numerous as the atoms of ten buddha fields: "Sons of noble family, there is a maṇḍala called Source of All Tathāgatas, the king of the heart that completely protects the undetermined realms of sentient beings. By relying on it, bodhisattvas will attain the ground of a tathāgata with little hardship, and will also directly realize the inconceivable dharmadhātu."

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi and the other vajra holders, as numerous as the atoms of ten buddha fields, said with one voice: "Blessed One, now is the time for this; Sugata, now is the occasion for this. Excellent, Blessed One! Out of love for us, may the Blessed One please explain the empowerment of the arrangement of the great maṇḍala, whatever it may be.

Lord of the Dharma, sovereign of the Dharma,
Sovereign of the Dharma, free of Antaka (Death),
Teacher possessed of great wisdom,
You are the one and only sovereign of the grounds.
Your aims accomplished, your accumulations accomplished too,
You have passed beyond the burden and reached the end of existence.
Great king of physicians, you know reality.
In you there is no cessation, in you there is no annihilation;
You have entirely abandoned the two extremes.
Suchness and the true limit,
Free of elaboration, you have realized and understood.
You are refuge, abode, and island,
You are nirvāṇa and peace as well,
Liberation, free of fever,
Free of the taint of dust.
Free of expression and of all designation,
You utterly destroy all entities."

Thus all those vajra holders,
Their minds ever filled with joy,
Having rightly proclaimed his names,
Made their request to the Guide.
The omniscient one, the great hero, free of illness,
Having understood what they requested,
Said, "I shall explain the maṇḍala
Arising from the heart of great compassion."

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, bowed at the feet of the Blessed One and said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, what is the name of this maṇḍala?"

When he had asked this, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, "Vajrapāṇi, the name of this maṇḍala is Universally Displaying the Appearance of Buddhas in World Realms Where There Are No Buddhas."

Then again the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, has the Blessed One not said that the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas, are like space? In space there is no arising and no ceasing. Blessed One, if in the same way the tathāgatas too have no arising and no ceasing, then why, Blessed One, did you say, 'This is the appearance of buddhas in world realms where there are no buddhas'?"

The Blessed One said, "Lord of Secrets, when an emanated buddha has passed into parinirvāṇa, at that time, in those world realms, bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of bodhisattvas by way of secret mantra bring sentient beings to full maturity through the empowerment of the arrangement of the great maṇḍala."

When he had said this, the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, how wondrous! Those bodhisattva great beings who completely fulfill the aspirations of sentient beings, beings of differing faith whose dispositions are marked by attachment, aversion, delusion, dishonesty, deceit, and doubt, exactly according to their aspirations, should be known as doing what is extremely difficult."

The Blessed One said, "Vajrapāṇi, it should be known that those sons of noble family have not served just one buddha; rather, those sons of noble family have planted roots of virtue under many buddhas. They have also come forth through the three gateways of the illumination of the Dharma. What are the three? They are the way of illuminating the gateway of the approach of great compassion, the way of illuminating the gateway of the approach of the profound Dharma, and the way of making offerings to and honoring the tathāgatas. Those sons of noble family should be known to possess these three gateways of the illumination of the Dharma."

Then again the Blessed One, gazing with his two eyes of loving-kindness and great compassion upon the bodhisattvas dwelling in the great palace of the dharmadhātu, said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, listen to the characteristics of a master. First of all, the master is one who has generated the mind set on awakening, who practices the training of a bodhisattva, who is restrained by the bodhisattva vows, who is compassionate and of great faith, who abides in the profound Dharma, who is skilled in the way of the Perfection of Wisdom, who is skilled in the arts, and who is clean and careful in his craft.

Trained in the practices of secret mantra,
In mudrā, in maṇḍala, and in ritual action,
Having received authorization from the guru
To perform the work of making maṇḍalas;
One who has become free of doubt
Toward the Buddha, the Dharma, and the supreme assembly,
And strives in order to protect completely
The realms of sentient beings without exception:
Such a learned one is fit
To draw the maṇḍalas of the buddhas.
Complete in all the marks, steadfast,
Upright of mind and courageous,
Able to pacify obstructors,
Whether for himself or for his students;
One whose inclination is vast
Toward realizing the meaning of perfect truth,
Patient and without pride,
Who performs vast deeds
And has faith in what is vast;
Who, hearing the aspirations
Of the bodhisattvas, the protectors, and the buddhas,
Rejoices again and again
And is always delighted;
Who strives in the practice of secret mantra
And makes effort in reciting recitations;
Who entertains no idle talk or guile at all,
And does not look to worldly concerns:
A mantrin devoted to mantra
Who possesses these and other such qualities
Is honored by the Teacher as a master.
Or else, one who abides
In the isolation of all phenomena
Is also fit to reveal
The appearance of buddhas, of all the buddhas.
Or else, one who knows the three samayas
Praised by the perfect buddhas,
And has entirely abandoned all faults:
A mantrin abiding in these,
Even if he does not possess every quality,
Is also fit to draw the maṇḍala.
If he possesses all such qualities,
Through having seen the rite of the action
He is fit to perform all maṇḍalas,
Both worldly and transcendent.

When that master is entreated by another, if he knows that the student earnestly seeks the goal and has faith in him,

Having known this, the master
Should carefully examine the ground.
Adorned all around with lakes, pleasure groves,
And ponds,
With trees bearing fruit and flowers,
And likewise with shrubs,
With fine and delightful saplings
Bearing fruit and flowers;
A land in which there are
Geese, cranes, peacocks,
Swans, parrots, and mynahs:
Diligently cultivate that land and resort to it.
Mountains with flowers and fruit,
Pure and lovely ponds,
Flowing streams of sweet water,
And rivers that circle to the right:
There the intelligent mantrin
Should draw the maṇḍala for the increase of wealth.
A land that is very fertile,
Temples and houses,
Shrines of the gods and cattle pens,
A solitary tree, and also a crossroads:
For peaceful rites, the wise one
Should draw a peaceful maṇḍala.
Empty houses or charnel grounds,
Barren places fit for fierce rites,
Foul, rough, and frightening,
Likewise thickets, harsh and terrifying:
In places such as these
Draw the maṇḍala of fierce rites.
In the fierce, perform fierce rites;
In the peaceful, perform peaceful rites;
In the abundant, it is increase itself:
That is the rite of increase.
Or again, wherever
The mind surely comes to feel joy,
In that place one should duly undertake
The work of drawing the maṇḍala.
For one whose mind is utterly pure,
Who has abandoned doubt and the like,
All rites will thereby
Be accomplished, without any doubt.
Nonconceptuality, free of conceptual thought,
Is awakening,
And it is attained by abandoning
Conceptual thought, not by anything else.
Therefore, in every way,
The intelligent one, having abandoned doubt,
Wishing to benefit the whole world,
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala.
There is no place, nor is there any time;
There is no act, nor any agent.
In all phenomena
There is only dharmatā.
Fools, obscured by ignorance,
Bewildered by causes and conditions,
Not realizing that phenomena are empty,
Are always under the power of Māra.
Therefore the Teacher, possessed of wisdom,
Knowing through realization free of elaboration,
Has taught the immeasurable Dharma
That abandons all conceptual thought."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana entered into equipoise in the samādhi called King of the Array of the Ornaments of All Buddhas. As soon as the Blessed One had entered into equipoise, a light ray called Ornament of All Buddha Fields came forth from between his eyebrows. Having come forth, it illuminated everything. Having fully illuminated this buddha field, it made level as the palm of the hand all these fields adorned with the array of the flower-ground essence, which have arisen from the maturation of the tathāgatas, are richly adorned with divine portals, and whose features and the limits of whose qualities could not be fathomed even in hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of eons. Having thus empowered the inconceivable array of the tathāgatas, those light rays vanished into the crown of the head of the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi.

As soon as those light rays had vanished, Vajrapāṇi bowed at the feet of the Blessed One and said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, please explain the empowerment of the arrangement of the great maṇḍala."

When he had requested this, the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva great being Vajrapāṇi, "Vajrapāṇi, listen to the empowerment of the arrangement of the great maṇḍala."

Saying, "Blessed One, I shall do so," Vajrapāṇi listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One said this to him: "Vajrapāṇi, in the places proclaimed above, the size of the maṇḍala should be made from one cubit upward to as much as one yojana. It should not be made larger than one yojana."

There the ground should be level and smooth,
Well smeared with fragrant ointments;
Or else the mantrin should diligently
Smear it with clean clay.

Then, having recited the secret mantra of all actions one hundred and eight times over pegs eight finger-widths long, if one drives them in evenly at the four corners, the maṇḍala will be empowered so as to be wholly unassailable by anything at all: namaḥ smanta vajrāṇān haṃ koṭaya same mahācaṇḍali hūṃ phaṭ.

Then, on the next day, one should gather the requisites of the maṇḍala, make the preparations, protect them with the secret mantra of all actions or with noble Acala, and have them placed on the north side of the maṇḍala. Or else, having empowered them, one should place them oneself. Then, on the following day, the students should be accepted: one should accept those who have great faith, great cleanliness, who strive in the Dharma, who are of good family, and whose inclination is vast, whether one, two, three, or as many as one wishes. With the secret mantra of all actions, or of noble Acala, or of the Invincible One, one should tie three knots in a thread spun by a girl, or in some other thread, or even in kuśa grass.

Then, having consecrated it with mantra
A hundred times or even more,
So that the student's rites may be accomplished,
It should be tied on the left upper arm.
A tooth stick of udumbara wood,
Smeared with flowers, scents, and the like,
Very fine and well protected,
Eight finger-widths long, with a single tip,
Fine and pleasing:
The learned one should then give it.
Facing east or north,
They should chew the tooth sticks.
Then the intelligent one, not eating it,
Should toss it away without deliberating.
Wherever it falls,
Accordingly will accomplishment come from that.
Have them listen to the Dharma of the sūtras,
Such as the Perfection of Wisdom.
To make the students weary of saṃsāra,
Give a very excellent Dharma talk.
If they see good
Or bad dreams,
To examine their own fortune,
They should relate them to the guru.
Stūpas, monastic parks, temples,
Books and deities,
Wish-fulfilling jewels and precious cloth,
Or else finding flowers and fruit,
Swords, wheels, and lutes,
And the various substances of accomplishment:
Whoever dreams of obtaining these in a dream,
His accomplishment will be as he wishes.
Seeing the sun, the moon, fire, or weapons,
Likewise crossing the ocean,
Or riding upon elephants:
For him accomplishment is not hard to obtain.
From such good dreams
Supreme accomplishment may be inferred.
If one sees the opposite, dreams harsh
And frightening, there will be no accomplishment.
Donkeys, camels, and dogs,
Water birds, tigers, cats, and foxes,
People whose bodies are smeared with sesame oil,
Naked, coarse, and ugly:
Such bad dreams as these
The intelligent person should reject.
Or else, knowing things to be like dreams
And like a city of gandharvas,
The intelligent one should explain
That dreams are without essence.

Then Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, what is the cause and what is the condition by which bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra become firm of mind toward such dharmas from the very beginning?"

The Blessed One said, "It is like this: those sons of noble family possess great compassion and view all phenomena as utterly and completely passed into nirvāṇa, and they know the characteristics of ordinary beings to be mistaken and wholly devoid of characteristics. Therefore they are firm of mind and certain toward all of these."

Then again the Blessed One empowered the vast expanse of the inconceivable dharmadhātu and said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, listen to the empowerment of the arrangement of the great maṇḍala. First, the master, having cleansed himself, should stand in the direction of Nairṛti, the southwest, and divide up the ground of the maṇḍala. Having bowed to all the buddhas and bodhisattvas and recollected the completely pure dharmadhātu, he should gaze upon the empowerment of the infinite, vast, and boundless array. Having gazed, he should empower himself as Vajrasattva, and with a cord of five colors,

Or else the learned one may do so mentally,
The wise one should divide
The fixed places of the deities.
Perfectly square and even,
With four gates and with archways,
With wide and extensive gateway enclosures,
It is drawn with a completely pure mind.
With powder of the five precious substances,
Or else with rice powder,
Or else with incense or with ashes,
The mantrin should draw the maṇḍala.
Square, with four gates,
The western gate should be left open.
Then one should recollect the queen of vidyā-mantras,
The great protectress, the Invincible,
Or else recollect Acala.
Having recollected the perfect Buddha,
One goes to the direction of Nairṛti.
Then, having stretched out the cord,
One circles around and returns.
Then the intelligent one
Goes to the northwestern quarter.
There too, facing east,
He stretches the cord toward the east.
Thus, knowing the method of the cord,
One should always strike the line properly.
Then, seated in the center,
Recollecting the tathāgatas:
The Protector Sarvāpāyajaha,
The supreme among humans, Amitābha,
Likewise Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin,
The Sage Saṃkusumita,
The Victor Vairocana,
The Great Sage, Victor over Māra,
Likewise the one called Akṣobhya,
And the Buddha Dundubhisvara:
There too the ascetic,
Diligently, should mark out the three portions.
Thus the one skilled in the rites,
Having examined the ground well,
The intelligent bodhisattva
Should also recite the Mother of the Buddhas,
Or else some other profound sūtra
That sets out the grounds.
Having recited it, the intelligent one,
Having wafted incense all around,
And having bowed to the sons of the supreme Victor
And to the buddhas,
The learned one should draw images
Of all the buddhas, or their mudrās,
Or else anything else at all:
All of these should be drawn."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana said to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of Secrets, there is a practice for accomplishing the mode of the inconceivable dharmadhātu, endowed with which bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra will, with little hardship, liberate even the infinite realms of sentient beings from the ocean of existence."

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One:

"Blessed One, please explain the bases of practice which, when they are endowed with them, enable bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva by way of secret mantra to complete every rite and to please the tathāgatas as well."

When he had requested this, the Blessed One Vairocana empowered the vast mode of the dharmadhātu, and having empowered it with the vast basis of practice, he explained the excellent practice: "Lord of Secrets, in this, a universal monarch should draw a maṇḍala about one yojana in size. The site is a house made of fine sandalwood, the supreme uragasāra, hung with great bells and small bells and with canopies of jewels; perfumed with various incenses of fine aloeswood; encircled with threads of Jambu River gold; adorned above with nets of bells and small bells of various jewels; resounding with the sounds of various sweet-sounding drums. On the four sides there are various archways, hung with garlands of pearls, draped with cloth dyed in various colors, and embellished with various arrangements and ornaments; and it should be brightly illuminated with various jewel lamps.

"Then, while remaining amid the assembly of the tathāgatas, one should empower oneself as the completely pure dharmadhātu, or as focused on its form, and bind the mudrā of the source of phenomena or the samaya mudrā. Then one should have those who follow the essential nature of emptiness take up the vows of the buddhas and bodhisattvas and the awakening mind. Then, in order to purify the unsurpassed eye of wisdom, one should show the samaya.

"Here, the mudrās of the inner maṇḍala are these. In the east one should draw the mudrā of all the buddhas, a triangle resting on a lotus. Its secret mantra is this: namaḥ sarvva buddha bodhi satvānān a sarvva traṇa ki svāhā.

"To the right of the Blessed One one should draw the mudrā of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas, a victory banner of the wish-fulfilling jewel. Its secret mantra is: namaḥ smanta buddha bodhi satvānān samanta amṛte jvala svāhā.

"Below it one should draw the mudrā of Samantabhadra, a sword, and a small maṇḍala of his abode. The secret mantra is: namaḥ sarvva buddha bodhi satvānān vajra rāja māla svāhā.

"To the left of the mudrā of all the buddhas one should draw the mudrā of the wheel of Dharma. Below it one should draw the mudrā of the Blessed One Śākyamuni, an alms bowl and Dharma robes. On the right side of the Blessed One one should draw the mudrā of the Eye of Space, resembling a uṣṇīṣa, resting on a lotus. Below the Blessed Lady one should draw the mudrā of the Supreme Vajra Giver, a vajra placed on an utpala flower. To the north of noble Samantabhadra one should draw the mudrā of Maitreya, a water flask. To the west of the Blessed One one should draw the mudrā of Avalokiteśvara, a lotus. To the north of the Blessed One one should draw the mudrā of King Acala, radiating infinitely and vast. To the south of the bodhisattvas one should draw the mudrā of all the vajra holders.

"Then the outer maṇḍala is made in three parts, leaving a strip for the circumambulation path. In the east one should draw the mudrā of all the hosts of messengers. In the southeast corner from them one should draw the mudrā of all the female messengers; the mudrā of the female messengers is a hook, and the mudrā of the hosts is a noose. On the southern and northern sides one should draw the mudrās of the male and female servants: the mudrā of the male servants is a sword, and for the mudrā of the female servants one should draw a dagger and a vajra needle.

"Then one should recite the Blessed Mother, the Perfection of Wisdom. Having recited it, one should rise, circumambulate the maṇḍala, and bow to the buddhas, the blessed ones. Then the students, who have generated the mind set on awakening, who have faith, great cleanliness, who are firm in their commitments, who have taken the vows, whose intention is to protect all beings completely, who are greatly compassionate, whose faces are covered with a cloth, whose hands are not empty, and who focus their minds one-pointedly on the maṇḍala, should circumambulate the maṇḍala and stand at the gate of the second maṇḍala. Binding the mudrā of fullness, the mudrā of the source of phenomena, or the samaya mudrā, and reciting the samaya, one should lead them into the maṇḍala. Then one should have them cast a flower. Wherever the flower falls, that is their deity.

"Thus the one who is firm in the samaya should generate toward the master the perception that he is the Teacher, and the master too should generate the perception that the student is his child. Then, having observed whether their mental continuum is superior, middling, or inferior, one should give them the yoga rite for entering the practice of secret mantra. If this is done, they will see the samaya in this arrangement of the great maṇḍala. Whether or not they have entered worldly and transworldly maṇḍalas, they will have entered the samaya. One who has thus been empowered in the great maṇḍala, who is free of all evil, who has been born from the family of the tathāgatas, and who keeps in mind the complete protection of the infinite realms of sentient beings, should recollect the yoga rite of the practice of secret mantra."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana gazed with his two eyes of great loving-kindness and great compassion upon the bodhisattva great beings seated in the abode of the dharmadhātu, and smiled slightly. Wishing to teach the unsurpassed, utterly pure dharmadhātu by way of entering the discriminating knowledges of the tathāgatas, he laughed a great laugh called Vowing to Deliver from the Whole Ocean of Existence, and from the maṇḍala of his great uṣṇīṣa a light ray called Source of the Domain of All Tathāgatas went forth in every direction. Having gone forth, that light ray, throughout the boundless world realms of the ten directions that pervade the expanse of space, illuminated all the bodhisattvas seated in the maṇḍalas of the assemblies of all the tathāgatas, and showed them the domain of entering the inconceivable dharmadhātu. It gazed upon the mental conduct of all sentient beings and took as its object the infinite, completely pure dharmadhātu. It sported by way of the sport that teaches the sameness of phenomena, following the sport of the dharmadhātu, which is equal to space. With a wind maṇḍala of devotion, accomplished from the inconceivable maturation of the roots of virtue of the tathāgatas and accomplished from the intention of the tathāgatas, it richly adorned the buddha fields with ten kinds of array; and, having arranged the empowerment of the maṇḍala of the great array upon the surface of space, it remained there.

As soon as those great light rays had come to rest, this thought arose in the mind of the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "How wondrous it is that these light rays, arisen from the intention of the Tathāgata and displaying arrays never seen before, have covered the surface of space with various arrangements, have displayed the great sports of the tathāgatas, and have then vanished into the face of the Tathāgata! Therefore, surely the Blessed One now wishes to explain the Dharma discourse on the sport of all the tathāgatas, the completely pure basis for entering sameness through the pure mode of accomplishing the great discriminating knowledge. Therefore I shall ask the Blessed One."

Then Vajrapāṇi bowed at the feet of the Blessed One and said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, from the time the Blessed One first generated the mind set on unsurpassed, perfect, and complete awakening until the Blessed One became manifestly and perfectly awakened, I have always been in attendance. Yet never before have I seen such a wonder as this: the Blessed One first laughing a great laugh as now, and then from the maṇḍala of the uṣṇīṣa on his head thousands of light rays going forth, as many as the particles in the atoms of ten buddha fields, and those light rays illuminating the world realms bounded by the limits of the expanse of space. Therefore I think, 'Since the Blessed One now wishes to explain to us the Dharma discourse that is the completely pure basis of the mode of accomplishing entry into the great discriminating knowledge, the manifestation of the perfect and complete awakening of all the tathāgatas, we have gained what is truly worth gaining.' Therefore, Blessed One, excellent! Out of love for us, please explain the Dharma discourse on the manifestation of the perfect and complete awakening of all the tathāgatas, the completely pure basis of the mode of accomplishing entry into the great discriminating knowledge. It will bring illumination to the beings of future times as well."

When he had requested this, the Blessed One said this to the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi: "Excellent, Lord of Secrets, excellent! That you ask about this matter is excellent indeed. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, listen well and keep it thoroughly in mind, and I shall explain to you the Dharma discourse on the manifestation of the perfect and complete awakening of all the tathāgatas, the completely pure basis for entering sameness in the pure mode of accomplishing entry into the discriminating knowledges of all the tathāgatas."

Saying, "Blessed One, I shall do so," the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One said this to him: "Lord of Secrets, the basis of emptiness is the basis of perfect and complete awakening. The basis equal to space is the basis of perfect and complete awakening. The basis of characteristiclessness is the basis of perfect and complete awakening. The basis of the tathāgata is the basis of perfect and complete awakening. In this way, a bodhisattva should cultivate these bases of perfect and complete awakening. Lord of Secrets, this is the Dharma discourse on the basis for entering sameness through the pure mode of accomplishing entry into the discriminating knowledges of all the tathāgatas."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana empowered the vast, boundless, and infinite dharmadhātu, and entered into equipoise in the samādhi called King of Stainless Space, Maturation Vast and Infinite like Space.

As soon as the Blessed One had entered equipoise, a ray of light called Illuminating the Domain of All the Tathāgatas, Revealing the Infinite Expanse of Inconceivable Domains, came forth from the crown of his head. Having come forth, it showed its accord with all the tathāgatas, circled the Blessed One Vairocana many hundreds of thousands of times, lit up the world systems of the ten directions with a great radiance, and stayed in the sky above. It set out the great maṇḍala of the secret essence, and with the light of the three great samayas it illuminated the world systems to the very limits of the expanse of space. It blessed the realms of sentient beings so that the sprouts of the three samayas would grow in them, and then the rays of light descended from the sky and alighted on the crown of Vajrapāṇi's head.

The very instant the rays of light alighted on the crown of Vajrapāṇi's head, the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi attained the great samādhi called Arising of the Domain of the Tathāgata, and through attaining it he came to abide nondually on the ground of the tathāgatas. Then the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, his mind satisfied with the joy of the tathāgata's wisdom, gazed for a moment at the Blessed One Vairocana with unblinking eyes, smiled slightly, and asked the Blessed One with his mind:

How is one a tathāgata?
How is one a supreme victor?
How is one a bodhisattva?
And how is one called a buddha?
Protector, I pray that you, great sage,
Dispel this doubt of mine.
Then the bodhisattvas of great renown,
Having cast off doubt and hesitation,
Those sons of the victors, will practice
The Great Vehicle, the king of conduct.

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, it is excellent, excellent, that you think to ask about such a matter. Lord of Secrets, rare are the beings who, on hearing such a basis for accomplishing the way of the Dharma, are not frightened and not terrified. Lord of Secrets, know that those sons of good family have not served just one buddha, but have planted roots of virtue under many thousands of buddhas. Therefore, Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra should first of all fully understand the realization of the Great Vehicle. Why is that? Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva who has fully understood the realization of the Great Vehicle will accomplish the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra with only slight difficulty. For this practice of the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra is the thorough purification of the great superknowledges."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, therefore listen, and keep it well and thoroughly in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

When he had said this, the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi listened to the Blessed One, and the Blessed One spoke as follows:

"Phenomena have the characteristic of space;
They are nondual, of a single characteristic.
One perfectly awakened through the ten bases
Is called a perfectly awakened buddha.
Because even mere perception is abandoned,
His own nature cannot be shown,
And he is self-aware through wisdom:
He is said to be a tathāgata.
Awakening has the characteristic of space;
It is the abandonment of all conceptions.
Whoever wishes to realize it
Is called a bodhisattva.
Having accomplished the ten grounds,
He fully comprehends the masteries.
Phenomena are empty, like an illusion:
One who fully realizes this here,
And knows the mindstreams of all the world,
Is called a buddha.

"Lord of Secrets, these, then, are the establishment of the states of the bodhisattva's mind. Lord of Secrets, by this enumeration too, it should be known that bodhisattvas do not accomplish any fixed establishment of their own mind."

Then again the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, that the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha has so well explained to the bodhisattva great beings the Dharma discourse on the realization of the Great Vehicle is wonderful, Blessed One. It is most wonderful, Sugata. Blessed One, I do not say that those sons of good family, while they circle in the saṃsāra of the five destinies, have any fear of the bad destinies and the lower realms, any fear of the works of Māra, or any fear of being born in unfavorable states. Why is that? Because the great bodhisattva who abides in the realization of the Great Vehicle, although he lives together with the world, does not give up the path that transcends the world, and in order to bring sentient beings to maturity he also displays, through skillful means, all the activities of the world. Nor does he ever fall away from the realization of the Great Vehicle."

Then again the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi made this request to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, please explain the maṇḍala of the great secret essence. It will bring benefit and happiness to many people."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, therefore listen, and keep it well and thoroughly in mind, and I shall explain to you the setting out of the maṇḍala of the secret essence.

"First of all, the master is skilled in distinguishing the three samayas, and he himself abides in the three samayas. Lord of Secrets, what, then, are these three samayas? They are three distinctive dharmas: the distinctive dharma that, in the times belonging to the three times, operates in an unobstructed way is called the three samayas. Lord of Secrets, what, then, is that distinctive dharma? Lord of Secrets, mind is the distinctive dharma. Lord of Secrets, in this, the thorough knowledge that all conceptualizations of mind are absent is the seeing of the perfect buddha. The aspect of self-awareness that realizes its dharmadhātu is the characteristic of the tathāgata."

"Whatever engages body, speech, and mind is the characteristic of emanation. Lord of Secrets, these are the three samayas. Since even all the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas do not realize them, what need is there to mention the other tīrthikas?"

Then the Blessed One spoke these verses:

"Childish beings obscured by ignorance,
Who have not come to know this way,
Seek the buddha somewhere else;
They do not realize that he dwells right here.
In no other world system whatever
Could a buddha ever be found.
Mind itself is the perfect buddha;
No buddha other than this has been taught.
Therefore the yogin,
Always casting off doubt,
Should examine the three kinds of distinction of mind;
Then he will know the three samayas."

Then again, having spoken these verses, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, in this, the master who is skilled in knowing the three samayas should resolve that he himself is the dharmadhātu, and through a practice that takes it as its focus he should purify the ground. Having thus purified the ground, by fully inclining toward what is like the expanse of space, he should bless himself as Vairocana, thinking, 'I am the domain of the unobstructed wisdom of the dharmadhātu.' Having thus blessed himself, for the sake of the purity of the dharmadhātu, he should, through the practice of nonduality, bless it so that the maṇḍala is in his own body, or so that the maṇḍala is his own body. Then, with powders of five colors made by his own mind, or others will do as well, he should lay it out according to the earlier rite. Lord of Secrets, this is in order to protect suffering sentient beings. Lord of Secrets, one should perform the rite of entering the samaya of the essence."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana performed such a blessing of the Dharma that through it bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra would attain even the wisdom of the tathāgata in this single life alone.

Then the Blessed One, having looked upon the realm of sentient beings with his two eyes of great love and great compassion, said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, listen to the basis of the blessing of the dharmadhātu, the basis for accomplishing blessing through which the omniscient wisdom of the tathāgatas, hard as it is to attain, will be firmly set even in the mindstreams of others, sentient beings who have not made merit."

When the Blessed One had said this, Vajrapāṇi joined his palms and said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, wonderful is the mastery of wisdom of the buddhas, the blessed ones, who possess the strength of the ten powers, the leaders of the herd who roar the lion's roar of the four fearlessnesses. Blessed One, what son or daughter of good family of wise nature would not endure, for the sake of such omniscient wisdom, even the unbearable sufferings of the hell beings for eons bounded only by the limits of the expanse of space? Blessed One, therefore, the wise son of good family who wishes to train in this will train in following the Tathāgata. Blessed One, therefore, out of your kindness toward us, please explain those bases of the blessing of the dharmadhātu well."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, because they are the same as the characteristic of space, all phenomena have a single characteristic. Because the dharmadhātu cannot be apprehended, all phenomena have the characteristic of the blessing of emptiness. Lord of Secrets, these are the bases of the blessing of the dharmadhātu that I cultivated and practiced many times, from the time I practiced the bodhisattva conduct until I sat at the seat of awakening. Lord of Secrets, therefore, that is the unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening of the tathāgatas. Son of good family, when I was seated at the seat of awakening, through these I attained the door of the Perfection of Wisdom called Abandoning All Conceptualization of Mind and Mental Factors. I also attained the dhāraṇī door called Proclaiming Infinite Sounds and Melodies, and the queen of vidyā-mantras called Melody That Defeats the Four Māras, which has no abode, is the domain of space, is without characteristics, without a goer, without an agent or an experiencer, and transcends all the courses of the world.

"Lord of Secrets, what, then, is that great queen of vidyā-mantras, infinite, boundless, and vast, that is to be taught?

namastraiyadhvīkānāṃ buddhagatepratiṣṭhitebhyaḥ apratihataśāsanebhyaḥ mahākṛpebhyaḥ gaganaamalarūpadhāraṇītathārūpavibhavavibhavena pararūpadharemahākṛpevimale sarvvagateviśodhani

"Here, this is the incomparable vajra word:

amahāvismayehūṃkhaṃ

"Vajrapāṇi, this is the queen of vidyā-mantras called Stainless like Space, blessed with the blessing of all the tathāgatas, the mother of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas. Son of good family, I have never seen a buddha field in which the tathāgatas do not explain and recite this queen of vidyā-mantras. Lord of Secrets, by this enumeration too, it should be known that a bodhisattva practicing the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra who wishes to enter this maṇḍala of the secret essence, its mudrās, and its samaya should apply himself zealously to this king of the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra."

Then the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, how do bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra show the nature of secret mantra? How do they progress, up to attaining mastery over all phenomena?"

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi:

"Lord of Secrets, in this, a bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra should receive empowerment from a master. He should give rise to the thought, 'This master is my teacher, who urges me on toward unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening.' Then he should approach with palms joined and make his request. The master, for his part, knowing that he seeks this aim, should make great compassion manifest and speak these words: 'Son of good family, arise! I shall show you the unsurpassed eye of the tathāgata.'

"Then, having examined a pleasing site and purified the ground according to the earlier rite, he should draw a square maṇḍala with a single door, in which the places for distinguishing the deities are well determined, furnished with an array of various places of offering, with a vase filled with sweet-smelling scented water set in the center. The disciples are seated upon lotuses, abiding in the visualization that the form of their own deity is themselves, their minds become the awakening mind, intent on protecting all beings without exception, and wishing to be born into the family of the tathāgata. With that vase he empowers them from the crown of the head. He should also say, 'Son of good family, you are born into the family of the tathāgata. The eye of the tathāgata is opened.'

"Lord of Secrets, in this, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra should know that the form of the deity, whether it be as many bodies of the tathāgata as there are, or the particular guises of all the infinite sattvas, comes about through blessing by secret mantra and mudrā. Lord of Secrets, and what is blessing by secret mantra and mudrā like? Lord of Secrets, at the time of empowerment, the master, with the lotus mudrā, should set out a seat, conceived as firm, stable, inconceivable, and indestructible like a vajra, made of gold, copper, or clay, or else drawn as a picture. For the one seated upon it, in order that he may gain mastery over all phenomena, he should confer empowerment from the crown of the head with a vase filled with sweet-smelling scented water. The master himself should also resolve upon it in a corresponding way, or perform the corresponding act with its mudrā.

"With the mudrā and the secret mantra of the Dharma conch, he should bless the maṇḍala of the wheel of the tathāgata's speech. With the mudrā and the secret mantra of the sword, he should bless the destruction of all views of the perishable collection. With the mudrā and the secret mantra of the tathāgata's wheel of Dharma, he should bless the tathāgata's wheel of Dharma. With the mudrā and the secret mantra of the vajra, he should bless the destruction of the city of ignorance. With the mudrā and the secret mantra of the uṣṇīṣa, he should bless the crown whose summit is invisible. With the mudrā and the secret mantra of the ūrṇā, he should bless the possession of the ūrṇā. With the mudrā and the secret mantra that arise from all the limbs, he should bless all the limbs.

"Lord of Secrets, in this, the lotus mudrā is formed thus: join the two hands together, open them out from the wrists, and stretch out all the fingers with their tips even. This mudrā is the vajra seat of all the tathāgatas, supreme and of great power. This secret mantra is the door of all great marvels:

namaḥsmantabuddhānān gaganāmalaprabhāvajrābhedyanāmahakṛpātmakaḥ

"Here, these are the incomparable vajra words:

ativismayamahākṛpātmajaḥhūṃ

"Cross the two hands, join them back to back, and interlace the fingers as though locked with one another; turn the left thumb back, and join it with the right thumb at the nail.

This seal is the great mudrā,
Proclaimed as the wheel of Dharma.
The protectors of the world, the saviors,
Turn this supreme wheel.

namaḥsmantabuddhānān tathāgataamṛtevijayavarṇanasarvvajayagatadharthakārimahākṛpehūṃhaṃtrahimahādbhūtehūṃ

"From the secret mudrā, place the two thumbs in the palms, wrap the two index fingers around their bases, and raise the rest, keeping them in place.

This mudrā of the world's protectors, the saviors,
Which always swiftly accomplishes
The aims of all sentient beings,
Is renowned as the Dharma conch.

namaḥsmantabuddhānān tyadadhvabhagavatipratiṣṭhāte anantarusaravinirghoṣaṃ sarbatathāgatavākprasaravismayakadharamahādharamahādharamaṣkasarvvasatvasantośanārāmahāmahāhūṃ

"From that same secret mudrā, bend the two index fingers and hold them on top of the thumbnails in the manner of a sword.

This mudrā of very great power
Is renowned as the sword mudrā.
It severs even the innate
View of the perishable collection in all beings.

namaḥsmantabuddhānān amahākhaḍgaprajñātejitasatkāyadṛṣṭicchindanakāra tathāgatādhimuktinirjātavidhutasaṃkalpamahāmahāhūṃjaḥ

"From that same mudrā, stretch the two index fingers outward and hold them at the third joint of the two middle fingers, about a barleycorn's breadth apart, without touching, making it like a vajra; place the thumbs, the ring fingers, and the little fingers inside.

This mudrā, proclaimed
By the buddhas, protectors of the world,
Is renowned as the vajra mudrā,
Which destroys the city of ignorance.

Its secret mantra is:

namaḥsmantabuddhānān mahāvismayatejodbhavamahācaṇḍadurdantātrabhañjahā

"From that same vajra mudrā, hold the two index fingers against the sides of the two middle fingers, in the shape of an uṣṇīṣa.

This is the uṣṇīṣa mudrā;
It is the protector of the world, the savior.
For whoever binds this mudrā,
This becomes the uṣṇīṣa.

Its secret mantra is:

namaḥsmantabuddhānān sarvvatraapratihatabhavatidharmmatābalinān sarvvatathāgatanodudharyabaśaya mahābudhāsarvvatathāgata adhiṣṭhānaadhiṣṭhātesphuramahāmahoṣṇīṣasvāhā

"Make a fist with the right hand and hold it between the eyebrows.

This seal is always proclaimed
As the ūrṇā of the supreme victors.

Its mantra is:

namaḥsmantabuddhānān aśikhendubamaprabhasarvvasatvakleśasantapapraśamanāgaramahātidhara sarvvatathāgataalaṃkāramahāpuruṣarūpadharakakotigatasvāhā

"Lord of Secrets, whatever it is through which bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra abide close to the activity of the buddhas, it is these secret mantras and mudrās. Lord of Secrets, therefore, bodhisattvas who are blessed by these secret mantras and mudrās of the bodhisattvas by way of secret mantra, with bodies like a wish-fulfilling jewel of various forms, display in the realms of sentient beings bodies that accord with whatever is needed to tame sentient beings."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, in the maṇḍala of the secret essence there is the vow, and the awakening mind: possessing that vow, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra come to practice with body, speech, and mind restrained. Lord of Secrets, what, then, is the vow? Restraint is the vow: that which gathers in and thoroughly gathers in body, speech, and mind, and which dispels outer and inner suffering, is the vow."

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, how should a bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra take up the vow?"

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, in this, the master should invite the deities, recite the Blessed Mother, the Perfection of Wisdom, and sit there facing the maṇḍala. He should call the disciples and instruct them thus: 'Child, receive the vow of entering the great samaya, which abides without obscuration in the three times.' The disciple, for his part, should join his palms, kneel, and say, 'Master, please heed me. I, who am called such-and-such, from this very moment today until I reach the seat of awakening, request the master to bestow upon me the vow of entering the great samaya. I wish to receive the command of the Teacher upon the crown of my head.' Then the master should also say this: 'Son of good family, consider! You who desire unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening should, until you sit at the seat of awakening, offer yourself with superior intention to the buddhas and bodhisattvas.'

"Lord of Secrets, this is the vow called entering the great samaya. Bodhisattvas who abide in this vow and practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra are thoroughly restrained in moral discipline and firmly established in the unsurpassed moral discipline of the tathāgata. Lord of Secrets, therefore, a bodhisattva who wishes to train in this door of the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra should apply himself zealously to this entering of the great samaya.

"Lord of Secrets, listen also to that awakening mind, possessing which a bodhisattva is born into the family of the tathāgata, and the wisdom of the past, future, and present buddhas, the blessed ones, becomes firmly established in him."

Then Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, out of your kindness toward us, please explain that awakening mind."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, what, then, is awakening? Awakening is the essential nature of space. Space, moreover, is formless, indemonstrable, and ungraspable. Whatever is the characteristic of awakening is also the characteristic of mind. Lord of Secrets, therefore, mind, space, and awakening are nondual. In this, the realization of phenomena as sameness, the comprehension of the dharmadhātu, is unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. In this, the arising of mind in the manner of non-arising and in the manner of non-apprehension, Lord of Secrets, is called the awakening mind.

"Lord of Secrets, moreover, a bodhisattva realizes awakening through five bases of abiding. What are the five? Lord of Secrets, in this, bodhisattvas realize it as free of all entities. Some realize it as empty, like an illusion, eliminating the aggregates, the elements, the sense bases, the grasped, and the grasper. Some do so by focusing on phenomena. Lord of Secrets, however, a bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra should realize it thus: free of all entities, eliminating the aggregates, the elements, the sense bases, the grasped, and the grasper, through the sameness that is the selflessness of phenomena, his own mind, unborn from the very beginning, is the essential nature of emptiness."

"Lord of Secrets, in this, 'free of all entities' is the Tathāgata's teaching of the complete abandonment of the worldly path, in that here there is no self, no sentient being, no birth, and no person, but only aggregates, elements, and sense bases. 'Eliminating the aggregates, the elements, the sense bases, the grasped, and the grasper' is the establishment of the bodhisattvas' realization of the Great Vehicle by way of thorough knowledge of the realization of the śrāvaka path. 'Through the sameness that is the selflessness of phenomena': through it, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra see their own mind as unborn from the very beginning, and realize it as the essential nature of emptiness, without substance and without characteristics. Lord of Secrets, therefore a bodhisattva should give rise to a mind that has no abode, no obscuration, and is free of all elaboration.

"Lord of Secrets, by this enumeration too, it should be known that, for bodhisattvas rightly established in the Great Vehicle, apart from the door of liberation that is the accomplishment of the way of practicing secret mantra, there is nothing that benefits unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. Why is that? That perfect and complete awakening which bodhisattvas fully awaken to after striving and seeking for many eons and doing even what is hard to give up, bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra will fully awaken to, as unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, in this very life. And why is that? Lord of Secrets, what is called 'life' is merely what the Tathāgata has us conceive of as birth. And that perception of those bodhisattvas ceases even before they attain the samādhi that comprehends the essential nature of all phenomena."

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana said to the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, a bodhisattva who wishes to enter this maṇḍala of the secret essence and the samaya of the mudrās should abandon five things far away. What are the five? Avarice, harming sentient beings, doubt, laziness, and disrespect toward secret mantras and the like. Lord of Secrets, these five things should be abandoned far away by bodhisattvas who wish to enter the supramundane path in this very life. Lord of Secrets, for the bodhisattva who abides in these things, I do not declare that even the slightest part of the conduct of awakening is brought to completion. Lord of Secrets, why is that? These things are great obstacles to the awakening mind of bodhisattvas."

Then the Lord of Secrets Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, how much will the merit increase of a son or daughter of good family who comes to abide in the samaya of this secret essence?"

When he had made this request, the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Lord of Secrets, can the limits of the expanse of space be known?"

He replied, "Blessed One, since the expanse of space has no measure, its measure cannot be taken."

The Blessed One said, "One might be able to know the limits of the expanse of space, but the measure of the merit generated by a son or daughter of good family who has seen the samaya in the maṇḍala of the secret essence cannot be known. Why is that? Lord of Secrets, this maṇḍala is seeing the appearance of a buddha. Lord of Secrets, what is called 'maṇḍala' is the chief essence, the culmination of all essences; there is no maṇḍala of the tathāgatas higher than it. It has become the essence of all roots of virtue, the abode, the refuge.

"Lord of Secrets, therefore a son or daughter of good family who wishes to enter this maṇḍala, mudrā, and samaya should go to wherever one may obtain entry into the maṇḍala, mudrā, and samaya, wading there on both feet even if the world system were completely filled with burning embers. Toward those bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra, those sons or daughters of good family should give rise to the perception of a spiritual friend, the perception of a father and mother, and the perception of a preceptor. Why is that? If one could not repay those bodhisattvas even by honoring them with one's own flesh and blood, how could one do so with any other offering? Lord of Secrets, how could one ever repay those bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of secret mantra, in reliance on whom the stream of hell beings is cut off, going to rebirth among animals is ended, and the aggregates belonging to the world of Yama cease to be? It should be known that those bodhisattvas abide as equal to the unequaled, are blessed with the blessing of all the tathāgatas, and have, like space, a domain without abode.

"Lord of Secrets, I shall instruct you; it should be known: in future times there will arise foolish people, who hold to objects of apprehension, attached to the view of the perishable collection, with distorted understanding, who have not settled the ground of their own minds, and who, confused, will say, 'This was not spoken by the Buddha; these were fabricated by fabricators.' Lord of Secrets, it should be known that those foolish people will go to two destinies. What are the two? They are the hells and the animal realm. Lord of Secrets, I do not declare that they have even the slightest cause in roots of virtue."

Why is that? Because they have set about cutting off the root. There will arise those overpowered by Māra, who create obstacles to the awakening of the buddhas, whose nature is corrupt wisdom, who have not trained themselves in the Great Vehicle, and who regard the Śrāvaka Vehicle as the essence. Toward the sons or daughters of good family who devote themselves to this Dharma discourse, they will speak abuse, saying, "How could those of corrupt moral discipline, who engage in evil things, have the awakening of a buddha?" Because those foolish people speak that abuse, they fall into the two lower destinies. For the conduct of practicing secret mantra was taught in this way by the Blessed One, the Omniscient One: he taught all of these for the sake of benefiting sentient beings. By giving rise to hostile thoughts toward them, they become ones whose lot is suffering. Why is that? It should be understood that those practitioners are holders of the secret of the tathāgatas, worthy of homage from the world together with its gods.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana addressed Vajrapāṇi and the other vajradharas, as many as the atoms of ten buddha fields: "In order that those sons of good family be guarded, protected and concealed, I entrust them into your hands, son of good family. You must guard those sons of good family constantly and without interruption. Protect them fully, and turn harm away from them."

When he had spoken thus, Vajrapāṇi and those other vajradharas, as many as the atoms of ten buddha fields, said these words with one voice: "Blessed One, Tathāgata, Lord of the Dharma, we shall do everything just as you have commanded. We shall guard those sons of good family until they reach the seat of awakening."

The Blessed One declared: "Excellent, excellent, sons of good family! Act in such a way that the teaching of the Teacher does not by any means perish."

Then the Lord of Secrets, Vajrapāṇi, said to all those vajradharas: "Friends, what the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas, accomplished through many hundreds of thousands of hardships is this unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. Therefore all of you, applying yourselves with diligence, must guard those practitioners. Why is that? Through this it will be said, 'We act according to the command of the tathāgatas.' It will be said, 'We guard the teaching of the tathāgatas.' It will be said, 'We are equal to the unequaled.' It will be said, 'We hold the ten powers.' Therefore we must always strive to guard, protect and conceal this Dharma discourse. And we must strive to guard, protect and conceal those sons of good family as well."

When he had said this, those vajradharas said with one voice: "We must certainly do this, so that by all means the teaching of the Teacher may remain for a long time and not perish."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, looking upon the beings of the future, so that this Dharma discourse might remain for a long time, and placing great compassion first, spoke these words of secret mantra:

namastraiyadhvikānān sarbbatathāgatānān sarbbatathāgatāsamatāgatigate ekaddharmmasambha sarbbasaraṇe vicitrarūpadhāriṇī sarbbasatatra abhiprāyabaripūraṇi sarbbabodhisatatragatamānase trahimaṃ sarbbaṅharmmasama mahāmahī vairocanagarbhasaṃbuddha sarbbatathāgata adhiṣṭhite trahite bhagavate hūṃ

Then Vajrapāṇi, authorized by all the tathāgatas and empowered by the blessing of the Blessed One Vairocana, made great compassion manifest and spoke the subsequent tantra of this very text:

First the master makes himself clean. Then, with a mind that has become the sphere of reality (dharmadhātu), he transforms himself into Vairocana through his mudrā and secret mantra, and draws the maṇḍala.
